- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the redacting plaintext type**

`internal/secrets/plaintext.go`:
```go
// Package secrets implements Alice's secret store: envelope encryption with
// per-secret data keys (DEKs) wrapped by the master key, sub-key derivation,
// and the recovery-passphrase backup key. Only this package decrypts secrets.
package secrets

import (
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"log/slog"
)

const redacted = "[redacted]"

// Plaintext holds decrypted secret material. Every formatting, JSON and
// logging path renders it as "[redacted]"; call Bytes to use the value and
// Zero when done.
type Plaintext struct {
	b []byte
}

// NewPlaintext wraps b without copying it.
func NewPlaintext(b []byte) Plaintext { return Plaintext{b: b} }

// Bytes returns the secret bytes. The caller must not log them.
func (p Plaintext) Bytes() []byte { return p.b }

// Zero overwrites the secret bytes in place.
func (p Plaintext) Zero() { clear(p.b) }

// String implements fmt.Stringer.
func (Plaintext) String() string { return redacted }

// GoString implements fmt.GoStringer.
func (Plaintext) GoString() string { return redacted }

// Format implements fmt.Formatter so every verb (%v, %+v, %#v, %s, %x, %q) is redacted.
func (Plaintext) Format(f fmt.State, _ rune) { _, _ = io.WriteString(f, redacted) }

// MarshalJSON implements json.Marshaler.
func (Plaintext) MarshalJSON() ([]byte, error) { return []byte(`"` + redacted + `"`), nil }

// LogValue implements slog.LogValuer.
func (Plaintext) LogValue() slog.Value { return slog.StringValue(redacted) }
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement envelope encryption, key check and derivation**

`internal/secrets/envelope.go`:
```go
package secrets

import (
	"crypto/aes"
	"crypto/cipher"
	"crypto/hkdf"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strconv"
)

// KeySize is the size in bytes of the master key and of every DEK (AES-256).
const KeySize = 32

const nonceSize = 12

// ErrDecrypt is returned for every decryption failure. It never says why,
// so it cannot be used as an oracle.
var ErrDecrypt = errors.New("secrets: decryption failed")

// ErrMasterKeyMismatch means the master key file does not belong to this database.
var ErrMasterKeyMismatch = errors.New("the master key does not match this database")

// HKDF info labels for sub-keys (SEC-043). The master key itself is never used as a MAC key.
const (
	LabelHWM       = "alice/hwm/v1"
	LabelJob       = "alice/job/v1"
	LabelAuditHMAC = "alice/audit-hmac/v1"
	LabelCSRF      = "alice/csrf/v1"
)

const keyCheckPlaintext = "alice-master-key-check-v1"

// Location names the database cell a secret lives in. It is bound into the
// AEAD additional data, so a ciphertext copied to another row or column
// fails to decrypt (SEC-041).
type Location struct {
	Table, RowID, Column string
}

func (l Location) payloadAAD() []byte {
	return []byte(l.Table + "/" + l.RowID + "/" + l.Column)
}

func (l Location) wrapAAD(version int) []byte {
	return []byte(l.Table + "/" + l.RowID + "/" + l.Column + "/" + strconv.Itoa(version))
}

// Envelope is the stored form of one secret (the *_ct, *_nonce,
// *_wrapped_dek and *_key_version columns).
type Envelope struct {
	Ciphertext []byte
	Nonce      []byte // 12 bytes
	WrappedDEK []byte // 12-byte nonce || AES-256-GCM(dek)
	KeyVersion int
}

// Keyring holds the current master key.
type Keyring struct {
	version int
	key     []byte
}

// NewKeyring returns a keyring for a 32-byte master key with the given version (>= 1).
func NewKeyring(version int, key []byte) (*Keyring, error) {
	if len(key) != KeySize {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("secrets: master key must be %d bytes, got %d", KeySize, len(key))
	}
	if version < 1 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("secrets: master key version must be at least 1, got %d", version)
	}
	return &Keyring{version: version, key: append([]byte(nil), key...)}, nil
}

// Version returns the master key version used for new envelopes.
func (k *Keyring) Version() int { return k.version }

// Seal encrypts plaintext with a fresh random DEK and wraps the DEK with the master key.
func (k *Keyring) Seal(loc Location, plaintext []byte) (Envelope, error) {
	dek := make([]byte, KeySize)
	rand.Read(dek)
	defer clear(dek)

	ct, nonce, err := seal(dek, plaintext, loc.payloadAAD())
	if err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	wct, wnonce, err := seal(k.key, dek, loc.wrapAAD(k.version))
	if err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	return Envelope{
		Ciphertext: ct,
		Nonce:      nonce,
		WrappedDEK: append(wnonce, wct...),
		KeyVersion: k.version,
	}, nil
}

// Open unwraps the DEK and decrypts the payload. Any mismatch (tampered
// bytes, other location, unknown key version) returns ErrDecrypt.
func (k *Keyring) Open(loc Location, env Envelope) (Plaintext, error) {
	if env.KeyVersion != k.version || len(env.WrappedDEK) <= nonceSize || len(env.Nonce) != nonceSize {
		return Plaintext{}, ErrDecrypt
	}
	dek, err := open(k.key, env.WrappedDEK[:nonceSize], env.WrappedDEK[nonceSize:], loc.wrapAAD(env.KeyVersion))
	if err != nil {
		return Plaintext{}, ErrDecrypt
	}
	defer clear(dek)
	if len(dek) != KeySize {
		return Plaintext{}, ErrDecrypt
	}
	pt, err := open(dek, env.Nonce, env.Ciphertext, loc.payloadAAD())
	if err != nil {
		return Plaintext{}, ErrDecrypt
	}
	return NewPlaintext(pt), nil
}

// Derive returns a 32-byte sub-key: HKDF-SHA256(master key, no salt, info=label).
func (k *Keyring) Derive(label string) []byte {
	out, err := hkdf.Key(sha256.New, k.key, nil, label, KeySize)
	if err != nil {
		panic("secrets: hkdf failed: " + err.Error()) // impossible for a 32-byte output
	}
	return out
}

func (k *Keyring) keyCheckAAD() []byte {
	return []byte("master_key_versions/" + strconv.Itoa(k.version) + "/key_check")
}

// KeyCheck returns a sealed known constant, stored in master_key_versions.key_check
// so a later start can tell whether the key file belongs to this database.
func (k *Keyring) KeyCheck() ([]byte, error) {
	ct, nonce, err := seal(k.key, []byte(keyCheckPlaintext), k.keyCheckAAD())
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return append(nonce, ct...), nil
}

// VerifyKeyCheck returns ErrMasterKeyMismatch unless check was made by KeyCheck with this key and version.
func (k *Keyring) VerifyKeyCheck(check []byte) error {
	if len(check) <= nonceSize {
		return ErrMasterKeyMismatch
	}
	pt, err := open(k.key, check[:nonceSize], check[nonceSize:], k.keyCheckAAD())
	if err != nil || string(pt) != keyCheckPlaintext {
		return ErrMasterKeyMismatch
	}
	return nil
}

func newGCM(key []byte) (cipher.AEAD, error) {
	block, err := aes.NewCipher(key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("secrets: %w", err)
	}
	return cipher.NewGCM(block)
}

func seal(key, plaintext, aad []byte) (ct, nonce []byte, err error) {
	g, err := newGCM(key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, nil, err
	}
	nonce = make([]byte, nonceSize)
	rand.Read(nonce)
	return g.Seal(nil, nonce, plaintext, aad), nonce, nil
}

func open(key, nonce, ct, aad []byte) ([]byte, error) {
	g, err := newGCM(key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if len(nonce) != g.NonceSize() {
		return nil, ErrDecrypt
	}
	return g.Open(nil, nonce, ct, aad)
}
```

### Task 5: Master key file bootstrap and recovery-passphrase backup key

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/secrets/masterkey.go`, `internal/secrets/backupkey.go`
- Test: `internal/secrets/backupkey_test.go`, `internal/secrets/masterkey_integration_test.go`
- Modify: `go.mod`, `go.sum` (`golang.org/x/crypto` becomes a direct dependency)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 4 `NewKeyring`, `Keyring.KeyCheck`, `Keyring.VerifyKeyCheck`, `ErrMasterKeyMismatch`, `ErrDecrypt`, `KeySize`, and the unexported `seal`/`open` helpers in `envelope.go`; Task 2 `db.Querier`; Task 3 table `master_key_versions(version, key_check, created_at, retired_at)` (app role has SELECT+INSERT) and `pgtest.Main`/`pgtest.New`.
- Produces (package `alice/internal/secrets`):
  - `const MasterKeyFile = "master.key"` (content: base64 StdEncoding of 32 bytes + `\n`)
  - `var ErrMasterKeyMissing`, `ErrMasterKeyPermissions`, `ErrMasterKeyInvalid` (messages as in the code below; wrapped errors name the path and the fix)
  - `func LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, dir string, now time.Time) (*Keyring, error)` — Task 16 calls it at startup with the app pool and `cfg.KeysDir`
  - `type KDFParams struct{ MemoryKiB uint32; Time uint32; Threads uint8 }` (JSON tags `memory_kib`, `time`, `threads`); `var BackupKDF = KDFParams{MemoryKiB: 64 * 1024, Time: 3, Threads: 4}`
  - `type WrappedBackupKey struct{ Version int; PublicKey, Salt, Nonce, Ciphertext []byte; KDF KDFParams }` with JSON tags `version, public_key, kdf, salt, nonce, ciphertext` — Task 12 stores it in settings `backup.key`
  - `func NewBackupKey(passphrase []byte, p KDFParams) (WrappedBackupKey, error)`; `func (w WrappedBackupKey) Unwrap(passphrase []byte) (*ecdh.PrivateKey, error)` (`ErrDecrypt` on a wrong passphrase or any tampering; KDF memory above 1 GiB is rejected before any work)

Behaviour of `LoadOrCreateMasterKey` (SEC-042, F-71, F-72):

| `master_key_versions` row | `master.key` file | Result |
|---|---|---|
| none | none | first start: create file `O_EXCL` mode 0400 (dir 0700), insert version 1 with key check |
| none | present | adopt the installer's file (permission and format checked), insert version 1 |
| present | none | `ErrMasterKeyMissing` — never generates a new key (it could not decrypt existing data) |
| present | present | mode must be 0400 or stricter (`ErrMasterKeyPermissions`, message says `chmod 0400 <path>`), then key check must match (`ErrMasterKeyMismatch`) |

`go.mod` note: `golang.org/x/crypto` (already an indirect dependency, in the spec's dependency budget) becomes direct for Argon2id in SEC-048; `go mod tidy` moves it.

`internal/secrets/backupkey_test.go`:
```go
package secrets_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/json"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"

	"alice/internal/secrets"
)

// fastKDF keeps unit tests quick; production uses secrets.BackupKDF.
var fastKDF = secrets.KDFParams{MemoryKiB: 64, Time: 1, Threads: 1}

func TestBackupKDFMatchesSpec(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Equal(t, secrets.KDFParams{MemoryKiB: 65536, Time: 3, Threads: 4}, secrets.BackupKDF)
}

func TestBackupKeyRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	pass := []byte("CANARY correct horse battery staple")
	w, err := secrets.NewBackupKey(pass, fastKDF)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, w.Version)
	assert.Len(t, w.PublicKey, 32)
	assert.Len(t, w.Salt, 16)

	priv, err := w.Unwrap(pass)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, w.PublicKey, priv.PublicKey().Bytes(), "unwrapped private key matches the stored public key")

	b, err := json.Marshal(w)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.NotContains(t, string(b), "CANARY")
	var back secrets.WrappedBackupKey
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(b, &back))
	_, err = back.Unwrap(pass)
	require.NoError(t, err, "survives a JSON round trip (stored in settings)")
}

func TestBackupKeyWrongPassphraseOrTampering(t *testing.T) {
	w, err := secrets.NewBackupKey([]byte("the right passphrase!"), fastKDF)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = w.Unwrap([]byte("the wrong passphrase!"))
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrDecrypt)

	swapped := w
	swapped.PublicKey = bytes.Repeat([]byte{1}, 32)
	_, err = swapped.Unwrap([]byte("the right passphrase!"))
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrDecrypt, "public key is bound by AAD")

	tampered := w
	tampered.Ciphertext = append([]byte(nil), w.Ciphertext...)
	tampered.Ciphertext[0] ^= 1
	_, err = tampered.Unwrap([]byte("the right passphrase!"))
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrDecrypt)
}

func TestBackupKeyRejectsHostileKDFParams(t *testing.T) {
	w, err := secrets.NewBackupKey([]byte("the right passphrase!"), fastKDF)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	w.KDF.MemoryKiB = 4 * 1024 * 1024 // 4 GiB
	_, err = w.Unwrap([]byte("the right passphrase!"))
	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "1 GiB")

	_, err = secrets.NewBackupKey([]byte("x"), secrets.KDFParams{})
	assert.Error(t, err)
}

func TestTwoBackupKeysDiffer(t *testing.T) {
	a, err := secrets.NewBackupKey([]byte("same passphrase value"), fastKDF)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	b, err := secrets.NewBackupKey([]byte("same passphrase value"), fastKDF)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.NotEqual(t, a.PublicKey, b.PublicKey)
	assert.NotEqual(t, a.Salt, b.Salt)
}
```

```go
//go:build integration

package secrets_test

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/base64"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"

	"alice/internal/secrets"
	"alice/internal/testutil/pgtest"
)

func TestMain(m *testing.M) { os.Exit(pgtest.Main(m)) }

var now = time.Date(2026, 9, 30, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

func versionRows(t *testing.T, d *pgtest.DB) int {
	t.Helper()
	var n int
	require.NoError(t, d.App.QueryRow(context.Background(), "SELECT count(*) FROM master_key_versions").Scan(&n))
	return n
}

func TestFirstStartCreatesKeyFileAndVersion(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	dir := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "keys")
	ctx := context.Background()

	k, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, dir, now)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, k.Version())

	info, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, secrets.MasterKeyFile))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, os.FileMode(0o400), info.Mode().Perm())
	dinfo, err := os.Stat(dir)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, os.FileMode(0o700), dinfo.Mode().Perm())
	assert.Equal(t, 1, versionRows(t, d))

	// A second start reuses the same key: data sealed before decrypts after.
	env, err := k.Seal(loc, []byte("persisted"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	again, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, dir, now.Add(time.Hour))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	pt, err := again.Open(loc, env)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "persisted", string(pt.Bytes()))
	assert.Equal(t, 1, versionRows(t, d))
}

func TestInstallerProvidedKeyFileIsAdopted(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	dir := t.TempDir()
	path := filepath.Join(dir, secrets.MasterKeyFile)
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(path, []byte(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(testKey(5))+"\n"), 0o400))

	k, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(context.Background(), d.App, dir, now)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	want, err := secrets.NewKeyring(1, testKey(5))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, want.Derive(secrets.LabelCSRF), k.Derive(secrets.LabelCSRF))
	assert.Equal(t, 1, versionRows(t, d))
}

// Review Focus #4: a new empty keys volume must never replace the key of existing data.
func TestMissingKeyFileWithExistingDataRefusesAndCreatesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	_, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, t.TempDir(), now)
	require.NoError(t, err)

	emptyVolume := t.TempDir()
	_, err = secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, emptyVolume, now)
	require.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrMasterKeyMissing)

	_, statErr := os.Stat(filepath.Join(emptyVolume, secrets.MasterKeyFile))

}

func TestDifferentKeyFileIsRejected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	_, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, t.TempDir(), now)
	require.NoError(t, err)

	_, err = secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, other, now)

}

func TestKeyFilePermissionsAndFormat(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	open := t.TempDir()
	path := filepath.Join(open, secrets.MasterKeyFile)
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(path, []byte(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(testKey(4))), 0o644))
	_, err := secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, open, now)
	require.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrMasterKeyPermissions)
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "chmod 0400")
	assert.Equal(t, 0, versionRows(t, d), "nothing recorded for a rejected file")

	bad := t.TempDir()
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(bad, secrets.MasterKeyFile), []byte("not base64 at all"), 0o400))
	_, err = secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, bad, now)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrMasterKeyInvalid)

	short := t.TempDir()
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(short, secrets.MasterKeyFile),
		[]byte(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte("sixteen byte key"))), 0o400))
	_, err = secrets.LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx, d.App, short, now)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, secrets.ErrMasterKeyInvalid)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the master key file handling**

`internal/secrets/masterkey.go`:
```go
package secrets

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"crypto/rand"
	"encoding/base64"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"alice/internal/platform/db"
)

// MasterKeyFile is the file name of the current master key inside the keys
// directory. Content: base64 (standard encoding) of 32 random bytes plus "\n".
const MasterKeyFile = "master.key"

var (
	// ErrMasterKeyMissing: the database holds encrypted data but the key file is gone.
	ErrMasterKeyMissing = errors.New("the master key file is missing but this database already holds encrypted data")
	// ErrMasterKeyPermissions: the key file is readable or writable by more than its owner (SEC-042).
	ErrMasterKeyPermissions = errors.New("the master key file permissions are too open")
	// ErrMasterKeyInvalid: the key file does not contain a base64-encoded 32-byte key.
	ErrMasterKeyInvalid = errors.New("the master key file is not valid")
)

const masterKeyVersion = 1

// LoadOrCreateMasterKey returns the keyring for the master key in dir.
//
//   - No master_key_versions row and no file: a first start. A new key is
//     written with mode 0400 (never over an existing file) and version 1 is recorded.
//   - No row but a file (created by an installer): the file is adopted.
//   - A row but no file: ErrMasterKeyMissing. A new key is never generated,
//     because it could not decrypt the existing data (F-71).
//   - A row and a file: permissions must be 0400 or stricter, and the key
//     must match the stored key check (ErrMasterKeyMismatch otherwise).
func LoadOrCreateMasterKey(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, dir string, now time.Time) (*Keyring, error) {
	path := filepath.Join(dir, MasterKeyFile)
	check, found, err := currentKeyCheck(ctx, q)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	_, statErr := os.Stat(path)
	fileExists := statErr == nil
	if statErr != nil && !errors.Is(statErr, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read master key file %s: %w", path, statErr)
	}

	switch {
	case found && !fileExists:
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: expected %s. Restore the key file from your backup; Alice will not create a new key because it could not decrypt existing data", ErrMasterKeyMissing, path)
	case found:
		k, err := loadKeyFile(path)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		if err := k.VerifyKeyCheck(check); err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s belongs to another installation or was replaced", ErrMasterKeyMismatch, path)
		}
		return k, nil
	case !fileExists:
		if err := createKeyFile(dir, path); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}
	k, err := loadKeyFile(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return k, recordVersion(ctx, q, k, now)
}

func currentKeyCheck(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier) ([]byte, bool, error) {
	var check []byte
	err := q.QueryRow(ctx,
		"SELECT key_check FROM master_key_versions WHERE version = $1", masterKeyVersion).Scan(&check)
	if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return nil, false, nil
	}
	if err != nil {
		return nil, false, fmt.Errorf("read master key versions: %w", err)
	}
	return check, true, nil
}

// recordVersion inserts the key check. When another process won the race,
// its row is verified against our key instead.
func recordVersion(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, k *Keyring, now time.Time) error {
	check, err := k.KeyCheck()
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if _, err := q.Exec(ctx,
		"INSERT INTO master_key_versions (version, key_check, created_at) VALUES ($1, $2, $3) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING",
		masterKeyVersion, check, now.UTC()); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("record master key version: %w", err)
	}
	stored, _, err := currentKeyCheck(ctx, q)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if err := k.VerifyKeyCheck(stored); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("%w: another process recorded a different key", ErrMasterKeyMismatch)
	}
	return nil
}

func createKeyFile(dir, path string) error {
	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("create keys directory %s: %w", dir, err)
	}
	key := make([]byte, KeySize)
	rand.Read(key)
	defer clear(key)
	enc := []byte(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(key) + "\n")
	defer clear(enc)

	f, err := os.OpenFile(path, os.O_WRONLY|os.O_CREATE|os.O_EXCL, 0o400) //nolint:gosec // G304: path is dir from configuration + fixed file name
	if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrExist) {
		return nil // another process created it first; it is loaded and verified next
	}
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("create master key file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	if _, err := f.Write(enc); err != nil {
		_ = f.Close()
		_ = os.Remove(path)
		return fmt.Errorf("write master key file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	if err := f.Sync(); err != nil {
		_ = f.Close()
		_ = os.Remove(path)
		return fmt.Errorf("sync master key file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	return f.Close()
}

func loadKeyFile(path string) (*Keyring, error) {
	info, err := os.Stat(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read master key file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	if perm := info.Mode().Perm(); perm&^0o400 != 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s has mode %04o; run: chmod 0400 %s", ErrMasterKeyPermissions, path, perm, path)
	}
	raw, err := os.ReadFile(path) //nolint:gosec // G304: path is dir from configuration + fixed file name
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read master key file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	defer clear(raw)
	key := make([]byte, base64.StdEncoding.DecodedLen(len(raw)))
	defer clear(key)
	n, err := base64.StdEncoding.Decode(key, bytes.TrimSpace(raw))
	if err != nil || n != KeySize {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s must contain a base64-encoded 32-byte key", ErrMasterKeyInvalid, path)
	}
	return NewKeyring(masterKeyVersion, key[:n])
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement the backup key pair**

`internal/secrets/backupkey.go`:
```go
package secrets

import (
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"crypto/rand"
	"fmt"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/argon2"
)

// KDFParams are the Argon2id parameters that turn the recovery passphrase
// into the key-wrapping key. They are stored next to the wrapped key.
type KDFParams struct {
	MemoryKiB uint32 `json:"memory_kib"`
	Time      uint32 `json:"time"`
	Threads   uint8  `json:"threads"`
}

// BackupKDF is the production setting from SEC-048 (m=64 MiB, t=3, p=4).
var BackupKDF = KDFParams{MemoryKiB: 64 * 1024, Time: 3, Threads: 4}

// maxKDFMemoryKiB bounds Argon2 memory so a hostile stored value cannot exhaust memory (1 GiB).
const maxKDFMemoryKiB = 1024 * 1024

const backupKeyAAD = "alice/backup-key/v1"

// WrappedBackupKey is the X25519 backup key pair: the public key in clear,
// the private key only wrapped by the recovery passphrase (R4, SEC-048).
// Neither the passphrase nor a passphrase-derived key is ever stored.
type WrappedBackupKey struct {
	Version    int       `json:"version"`
	PublicKey  []byte    `json:"public_key"`
	KDF        KDFParams `json:"kdf"`
	Salt       []byte    `json:"salt"`
	Nonce      []byte    `json:"nonce"`
	Ciphertext []byte    `json:"ciphertext"`
}

// NewBackupKey generates an X25519 key pair and wraps the private key with
// AES-256-GCM under Argon2id(passphrase).
func NewBackupKey(passphrase []byte, p KDFParams) (WrappedBackupKey, error) {
	if err := p.validate(); err != nil {
		return WrappedBackupKey{}, err
	}
	priv, err := ecdh.X25519().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	if err != nil {
		return WrappedBackupKey{}, fmt.Errorf("generate backup key: %w", err)
	}
	salt := make([]byte, 16)
	rand.Read(salt)
	kek := argon2.IDKey(passphrase, salt, p.Time, p.MemoryKiB, p.Threads, KeySize)
	defer clear(kek)

	pub := priv.PublicKey().Bytes()
	privBytes := priv.Bytes()
	defer clear(privBytes)
	ct, nonce, err := seal(kek, privBytes, append([]byte(backupKeyAAD), pub...))
	if err != nil {
		return WrappedBackupKey{}, err
	}
	return WrappedBackupKey{Version: 1, PublicKey: pub, KDF: p, Salt: salt, Nonce: nonce, Ciphertext: ct}, nil
}

// Unwrap recovers the private key. A wrong passphrase or any modified field returns ErrDecrypt.
func (w WrappedBackupKey) Unwrap(passphrase []byte) (*ecdh.PrivateKey, error) {
	if w.Version != 1 || len(w.Salt) == 0 || len(w.PublicKey) != 32 {
		return nil, ErrDecrypt
	}
	if err := w.KDF.validate(); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	kek := argon2.IDKey(passphrase, w.Salt, w.KDF.Time, w.KDF.MemoryKiB, w.KDF.Threads, KeySize)
	defer clear(kek)
	raw, err := open(kek, w.Nonce, w.Ciphertext, append([]byte(backupKeyAAD), w.PublicKey...))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrDecrypt
	}
	defer clear(raw)
	priv, err := ecdh.X25519().NewPrivateKey(raw)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrDecrypt
	}
	return priv, nil
}

func (p KDFParams) validate() error {
	if p.MemoryKiB == 0 || p.Time == 0 || p.Threads == 0 {
		return fmt.Errorf("secrets: backup key KDF parameters must be positive")
	}
	if p.MemoryKiB > maxKDFMemoryKiB {
		return fmt.Errorf("secrets: backup key KDF memory %d KiB exceeds the 1 GiB limit", p.MemoryKiB)
	}
	return nil
}
```

### Task 6: Audit log: hash chain, append, list, verify, checkpoint

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/audit/audit.go`, `internal/audit/query.go`, `internal/audit/verify.go`
- Test: `internal/audit/audit_test.go`, `internal/audit/audit_integration_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2 `db.Querier`, `db.InTx(ctx, pool, func(pgx.Tx) error) error`, `settings.Get(ctx, q, key, dst) (bool, error)`, `settings.Set(ctx, q, key, v, now) error`; Phase 0 `clock.Clock`, `clock.NewFake`; Task 3 table `audit_log` (app role: SELECT+INSERT only; append-only triggers), `pgtest.Main`/`pgtest.New` (`DB.App` = alice_app pool, `DB.Owner` = owner pool).
- Produces (package `alice/internal/audit`), exactly as follows:
  - `type ActorType string` (`ActorUser`, `ActorToken`, `ActorSystem`); `type Result string` (`Success`, `Failure`, `Denied`)
  - `type Actor struct{ Type ActorType; ID uuid.UUID; Name string; IP netip.Addr; RequestID string }`; `func SystemActor(name string) Actor`
  - `type Event struct{ Actor Actor; Action, ObjectType, ObjectID string; Result Result; Details map[string]string }`
  - `type Entry struct{ Seq int64; At time.Time; ActorType ActorType; ActorID uuid.UUID; ActorName, IP, Action, ObjectType, ObjectID string; Result Result; Details map[string]string; RequestID string; PrevHash, Hash []byte }`
  - `var ErrForbiddenDetailKey`; forbidden exact keys `password, secret, passphrase, token, code, totp_secret, private_key, recovery_code` and any key with prefix `private`
  - `type Log`; `func New(c clock.Clock) *Log`; `func (l *Log) Append(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, e Event) (Entry, error)`
  - `func ComputeHash(prev []byte, e Entry) []byte`
  - `type Filter struct{ Action string; Actor string; Result Result; BeforeSeq int64; Limit int }`; `func List(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, f Filter) ([]Entry, int64, error)`; `func Iterate(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, fn func(Entry) error) error`
  - `const ProblemHashMismatch = "hash_mismatch"`, `ProblemSequenceGap = "sequence_gap"`, `ProblemTailTruncated = "tail_truncated"`; `type Report struct{ Intact bool; Checked, HeadSeq int64; HeadHash []byte; FirstBadSeq int64; Problem string }`; `func Verify(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier) (Report, error)`
  - `type Service`; `func NewService(l *Log, pool *pgxpool.Pool) *Service`; methods `Write(ctx, Event) error`, `List(ctx, Filter) ([]Entry, int64, error)`, `Iterate(ctx, func(Entry) error) error`, `Verify(ctx, Actor) (Report, error)` — this is the `api.AuditService` implementation (Task 13) and is used by the CLI `alice audit verify` (Task 16)
  - Audit action written here: `audit.verified` (object_type `audit_log`; details `checked`, `head_seq`, plus `problem`, `first_bad_seq` on failure). Settings key `audit.checkpoint` = `{"seq":N,"hash":"<hex>","at":"<RFC3339>"}`.

Design notes (spec §7.5, SEC-070..072, F-90..F-97):
- `hash = SHA-256(prev_hash || canonical JSON)`, first `prev_hash` = 32 zero bytes. The canonical JSON is a fixed-order struct; `at` is UTC truncated to microseconds (PostgreSQL precision), `actor_id` is `""` for none, `details` is always an object (encoding/json sorts map keys), IPs are canonicalised with `netip.Addr.Unmap().String()` on write and on read (read with `host(ip)`), so the hash is identical after a round trip (Review Focus #1).
- `seq` is gapless and app-assigned: `Append` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(7160927315633811300)`, reads the head and inserts `max+1` in the caller's transaction. A rolled-back business transaction takes its audit row with it, so no gap appears; any gap is tampering.
- `Verify` walks the chain ascending; it reports the first `seq` whose number is unexpected (`sequence_gap`), whose `prev_hash`/`hash` does not recompute (`hash_mismatch`), or where the row at the stored checkpoint has another hash; if the checkpoint is beyond the head, the tail was deleted (`tail_truncated`). `Service.Verify` stores a new checkpoint only when the chain is intact.
- Tampering tests act as the table owner with the append-only triggers disabled inside a transaction — an attacker with owner rights. That the app role cannot UPDATE/DELETE at all (F-94) is tested in Task 3; here a test shows the triggers stop even the owner.
- The scheduled daily verification job and the critical alert on a broken chain need the job system and notifications (later phases); Phase 1 offers the API/CLI verification.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing unit tests**

`internal/audit/audit_test.go`:
```go
package audit_test

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"

	"alice/internal/audit"
)

func sample() audit.Entry {
	return audit.Entry{
		Seq:       1,
		At:        time.Date(2026, 9, 30, 10, 0, 0, 123456789, time.UTC),
		ActorType: audit.ActorUser,
		ActorID:   uuid.MustParse("0192e0a0-0000-7000-8000-000000000001"),
		ActorName: "admin",
		IP:        "10.0.0.7",
		Action:    "user.created",
		Result:    audit.Success,
		Details:   map[string]string{"b": "2", "a": "İzmir"},
		RequestID: "req-1",
	}
}

func TestComputeHashIsDeterministicAndTruncatesToMicroseconds(t *testing.T) {
	zero := make([]byte, sha256.Size)
	e := sample()
	h := audit.ComputeHash(zero, e)
	assert.Len(t, h, 32)

	same := sample()
	same.At = same.At.Truncate(time.Microsecond).In(time.FixedZone("TRT", 3*3600))
	same.Details = map[string]string{"a": "İzmir", "b": "2"}
	assert.Equal(t, h, audit.ComputeHash(zero, same), "time zone, sub-microsecond digits and map order do not matter")

	nilDetails := sample()
	nilDetails.Details = nil
	empty := sample()
	empty.Details = map[string]string{}
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ComputeHash(zero, nilDetails), audit.ComputeHash(zero, empty))
}

func TestComputeHashCoversEveryField(t *testing.T) {
	zero := make([]byte, sha256.Size)
	base := audit.ComputeHash(zero, sample())
	mutations := map[string]func(*audit.Entry){
		"seq":         func(e *audit.Entry) { e.Seq = 2 },
		"at":          func(e *audit.Entry) { e.At = e.At.Add(time.Microsecond) },
		"actor_type":  func(e *audit.Entry) { e.ActorType = audit.ActorSystem },
		"actor_id":    func(e *audit.Entry) { e.ActorID = uuid.Nil },
		"actor_name":  func(e *audit.Entry) { e.ActorName = "root" },
		"ip":          func(e *audit.Entry) { e.IP = "" },
		"action":      func(e *audit.Entry) { e.Action = "user.updated" },
		"object_type": func(e *audit.Entry) { e.ObjectType = "user" },
		"object_id":   func(e *audit.Entry) { e.ObjectID = "x" },
		"result":      func(e *audit.Entry) { e.Result = audit.Failure },
		"details":     func(e *audit.Entry) { e.Details["a"] = "Izmir" },
		"request_id":  func(e *audit.Entry) { e.RequestID = "req-2" },
	}
	for name, mutate := range mutations {
		e := sample()
		mutate(&e)
		assert.NotEqual(t, base, audit.ComputeHash(zero, e), name)
	}
	prev := make([]byte, sha256.Size)
	prev[0] = 1
	assert.NotEqual(t, base, audit.ComputeHash(prev, sample()), "prev hash is chained")
}

func TestSystemActor(t *testing.T) {
	a := audit.SystemActor("cli")
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ActorSystem, a.Type)
	assert.Equal(t, "cli", a.Name)
	assert.Equal(t, uuid.Nil, a.ID)
}
```

`internal/audit/audit_integration_test.go`:
```go
//go:build integration

package audit_test

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/hex"
	"fmt"
	"net/netip"
	"os"
	"strconv"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"

	"alice/internal/audit"
	"alice/internal/platform/clock"
	"alice/internal/platform/db"
	"alice/internal/platform/settings"
	"alice/internal/testutil/pgtest"
)

func TestMain(m *testing.M) { os.Exit(pgtest.Main(m)) }

var start = time.Date(2026, 9, 30, 9, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

func userActor() audit.Actor {
	return audit.Actor{Type: audit.ActorUser, ID: uuid.MustParse("0192e0a0-0000-7000-8000-00000000000a"),
		Name: "admin", IP: netip.MustParseAddr("192.0.2.10"), RequestID: "req"}
}

func appendN(t *testing.T, pool *pgxpool.Pool, l *audit.Log, n int) {
	t.Helper()
	for i := range n {
		require.NoError(t, db.InTx(context.Background(), pool, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
			_, err := l.Append(context.Background(), tx, audit.Event{Actor: userActor(), Action: "test.event",
				ObjectType: "thing", ObjectID: strconv.Itoa(i), Result: audit.Success, Details: map[string]string{"i": strconv.Itoa(i)}})
			return err
		}))
	}
}

// ownerExec runs tampering SQL as the table owner with the append-only triggers disabled,
// simulating an attacker with owner rights.
func ownerExec(t *testing.T, d *pgtest.DB, sql string, args ...any) {
	t.Helper()
	ctx := context.Background()
	require.NoError(t, db.InTx(ctx, d.Owner, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "ALTER TABLE audit_log DISABLE TRIGGER USER"); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, sql, args...); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		_, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "ALTER TABLE audit_log ENABLE TRIGGER USER")
		return err
	}))
}

func TestAppendBuildsAVerifiableChain(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	ctx := context.Background()

	rep, err := audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.Report{Intact: true}, rep, "an empty log is intact")

	appendN(t, d.App, l, 5)
	rep, err = audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(5), rep.Checked)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(5), rep.HeadSeq)

	var first []byte
	require.NoError(t, d.App.QueryRow(ctx, "SELECT prev_hash FROM audit_log WHERE seq = 1").Scan(&first))
	assert.Equal(t, make([]byte, 32), first, "first link starts from 32 zero bytes")
}

func TestRolledBackAppendLeavesNoGap(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	ctx := context.Background()
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 1)
	err := db.InTx(ctx, d.App, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		if _, err := l.Append(ctx, tx, audit.Event{Actor: userActor(), Action: "test.event", Result: audit.Success}); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		return fmt.Errorf("business change failed")
	})
	require.Error(t, err)
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 1)
	rep, err := audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(2), rep.HeadSeq)
}

// Review Focus #1: values that change shape in PostgreSQL still hash identically after a round trip.
func TestRoundTripOfUnusualValuesStaysIntact(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	fake := clock.NewFake(time.Date(2026, 9, 30, 9, 0, 0, 123456789, time.UTC)) // nanoseconds
	l := audit.New(fake)
	ctx := context.Background()
	details := map[string]string{"city": "İzmir", "district": "ığdır", "note": `quote " and <tag> & ünïcödé`}
	for i := range 20 {
		details[fmt.Sprintf("k%02d", 19-i)] = strconv.Itoa(i)
	}
	actor := audit.Actor{Type: audit.ActorUser, Name: "İsmail", IP: netip.MustParseAddr("::ffff:10.0.0.7"), RequestID: "r"}
	var written audit.Entry
	require.NoError(t, db.InTx(ctx, d.App, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		var err error
		written, err = l.Append(ctx, tx, audit.Event{Actor: actor, Action: "user.updated", Result: audit.Success, Details: details})
		return err
	}))
	fake.Advance(1500 * time.Nanosecond)
	require.NoError(t, audit.NewService(l, d.App).Write(ctx, audit.Event{Actor: audit.SystemActor("cli"), Action: "test.event", Result: audit.Failure}))

	items, _, err := audit.List(ctx, d.App, audit.Filter{})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, items, 2)
	got := items[1]
	assert.Equal(t, "10.0.0.7", got.IP, "IPv4-mapped IPv6 is stored in canonical IPv4 form")
	assert.Equal(t, details, got.Details)
	assert.Equal(t, written.Hash, got.Hash)
	assert.True(t, got.At.Equal(time.Date(2026, 9, 30, 9, 0, 0, 123456000, time.UTC)))

	rep, err := audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact, "%+v", rep)
}

func TestForbiddenDetailKeysAreRejected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	ctx := context.Background()
	for _, k := range []string{"password", "secret", "passphrase", "token", "code", "totp_secret", "private_key", "recovery_code", "private_anything"} {
		err := db.InTx(ctx, d.App, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
			_, err := l.Append(ctx, tx, audit.Event{Actor: userActor(), Action: "x.y", Result: audit.Success, Details: map[string]string{k: "v"}})
			return err
		})
		assert.ErrorIs(t, err, audit.ErrForbiddenDetailKey, k)
	}
	err := db.InTx(ctx, d.App, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		_, err := l.Append(ctx, tx, audit.Event{Actor: userActor(), Action: "token.created", Result: audit.Success, Details: map[string]string{"token_id": "t1"}})
		return err
	})
	assert.NoError(t, err, "keys that merely contain a word such as token_id are allowed")
}

// F-90: a modified row is reported at its sequence number.
func TestModifiedRowIsDetected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	appendN(t, d.App, audit.New(clock.NewFake(start)), 5)
	ownerExec(t, d, "UPDATE audit_log SET actor_name = 'mallory' WHERE seq = 3")
	rep, err := audit.Verify(context.Background(), d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(3), rep.FirstBadSeq)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ProblemHashMismatch, rep.Problem)
}

// F-91: a deleted middle row leaves a gap.
func TestDeletedMiddleRowIsDetected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	appendN(t, d.App, audit.New(clock.NewFake(start)), 5)
	ownerExec(t, d, "DELETE FROM audit_log WHERE seq = 2")
	rep, err := audit.Verify(context.Background(), d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(2), rep.FirstBadSeq)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ProblemSequenceGap, rep.Problem)
}

// F-92: deleting the newest rows is detected through the stored checkpoint.
func TestTruncatedTailIsDetectedByCheckpoint(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	svc := audit.NewService(l, d.App)
	ctx := context.Background()
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 4)
	rep, err := svc.Verify(ctx, audit.SystemActor("cli")) // checkpoint at seq 4, verify event becomes seq 5
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.True(t, rep.Intact)

	ownerExec(t, d, "DELETE FROM audit_log WHERE seq >= 4")
	rep, err = audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ProblemTailTruncated, rep.Problem)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(4), rep.FirstBadSeq)
}

func TestCheckpointHashMismatchIsDetected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	ctx := context.Background()
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 3)
	require.NoError(t, settings.Set(ctx, d.App, "audit.checkpoint",
		map[string]any{"seq": 2, "hash": hex.EncodeToString(make([]byte, 32)), "at": start.Format(time.RFC3339)}, start))
	rep, err := audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(2), rep.FirstBadSeq)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ProblemHashMismatch, rep.Problem)
}

// F-93: swapping the contents of two rows is detected.
func TestReorderedRowsAreDetected(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	appendN(t, d.App, audit.New(clock.NewFake(start)), 4)
	ownerExec(t, d, `UPDATE audit_log a SET object_id = b.object_id, details = b.details
		FROM audit_log b WHERE (a.seq, b.seq) IN ((2, 3), (3, 2))`)
	rep, err := audit.Verify(context.Background(), d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(2), rep.FirstBadSeq)
}

// Append-only triggers hold even for the owner role (F-94's privilege check is in Task 3).
func TestTriggersBlockUpdateDeleteTruncateForOwner(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	appendN(t, d.App, audit.New(clock.NewFake(start)), 1)
	ctx := context.Background()
	for _, sql := range []string{"UPDATE audit_log SET action = 'x'", "DELETE FROM audit_log", "TRUNCATE audit_log"} {
		_, err := d.Owner.Exec(ctx, sql)
		assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "append-only", sql)
	}
}

// F-95: 100 concurrent writers keep the chain contiguous and valid.
func TestConcurrentAppendsStayContiguous(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	svc := audit.NewService(audit.New(clock.NewFake(start)), d.App)
	ctx := context.Background()
	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	errs := make(chan error, 100)
	for i := range 100 {
		wg.Add(1)
		go func() {
			defer wg.Done()
			errs <- svc.Write(ctx, audit.Event{Actor: userActor(), Action: "test.concurrent", ObjectID: strconv.Itoa(i), Result: audit.Success})
		}()
	}
	wg.Wait()
	close(errs)
	for err := range errs {
		require.NoError(t, err)
	}
	rep, err := audit.Verify(ctx, d.App)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(100), rep.HeadSeq)
}

// F-97 style: after verification the chain continues from the verified head.
func TestServiceVerifyRecordsCheckpointAndEventThenChainContinues(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	svc := audit.NewService(l, d.App)
	ctx := context.Background()
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 3)

	rep, err := svc.Verify(ctx, audit.SystemActor("cli"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(3), rep.HeadSeq)

	var cp struct {
		Seq  int64  `json:"seq"`
		Hash string `json:"hash"`
	}
	ok, err := settings.Get(ctx, d.App, "audit.checkpoint", &cp)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(3), cp.Seq)
	assert.Equal(t, hex.EncodeToString(rep.HeadHash), cp.Hash)

	items, _, err := svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Limit: 1})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "audit.verified", items[0].Action)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.Success, items[0].Result)
	assert.Equal(t, "3", items[0].Details["checked"])

	appendN(t, d.App, l, 2)
	rep, err = svc.Verify(ctx, audit.SystemActor("cli"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, rep.Intact)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(6), rep.HeadSeq)
}

func TestServiceVerifyRecordsFailure(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	svc := audit.NewService(l, d.App)
	ctx := context.Background()
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 3)
	ownerExec(t, d, "UPDATE audit_log SET result = 'failure' WHERE seq = 2")

	rep, err := svc.Verify(ctx, audit.SystemActor("cli"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, rep.Intact)
	items, _, err := svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Action: "audit.verified"})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, items, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.Failure, items[0].Result)
	assert.Equal(t, audit.ProblemHashMismatch, items[0].Details["problem"])
	assert.Equal(t, "2", items[0].Details["first_bad_seq"])
	ok, err := settings.Get(ctx, d.App, "audit.checkpoint", &struct{}{})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, ok, "no checkpoint is stored for a broken chain")
}

func TestListPagingAndFilters(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	svc := audit.NewService(l, d.App)
	ctx := context.Background()
	events := []audit.Event{
		{Actor: userActor(), Action: "auth.login", Result: audit.Success},
		{Actor: audit.Actor{Type: audit.ActorUser, Name: "Operator1"}, Action: "auth.login", Result: audit.Failure},
		{Actor: userActor(), Action: "user.created", Result: audit.Success},
		{Actor: userActor(), Action: "auth_login_lookalike", Result: audit.Success},
		{Actor: userActor(), Action: "authz.denied", Result: audit.Denied},
	}
	for _, e := range events {
		require.NoError(t, svc.Write(ctx, e))
	}

	page, next, err := svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Limit: 2})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, page, 2)
	assert.Equal(t, []int64{5, 4}, []int64{page[0].Seq, page[1].Seq}, "newest first")
	assert.Equal(t, int64(4), next)
	page, next, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Limit: 2, BeforeSeq: next})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, []int64{3, 2}, []int64{page[0].Seq, page[1].Seq})
	page, next, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Limit: 2, BeforeSeq: next})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, page, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, int64(0), next, "no further page")

	page, _, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Action: "auth."})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, page, 2, "prefix match; '_' is not a wildcard")
	page, _, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Actor: "operator1"})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, page, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "Operator1", page[0].ActorName)
	page, _, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Result: audit.Denied})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, page, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "authz.denied", page[0].Action)

	page, _, err = svc.List(ctx, audit.Filter{Limit: 1000})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, page, 5, "limit is capped at 200, not rejected")
}

func TestIterateIsAscending(t *testing.T) {
	d := pgtest.New(t)
	l := audit.New(clock.NewFake(start))
	appendN(t, d.App, l, 3)
	var seqs []int64
	require.NoError(t, audit.NewService(l, d.App).Iterate(context.Background(), func(e audit.Entry) error {
		seqs = append(seqs, e.Seq)
		return nil
	}))
	assert.Equal(t, []int64{1, 2, 3}, seqs)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the event model, hashing and Append**

`internal/audit/audit.go`:
```go
// Package audit is Alice's append-only, hash-chained audit log (spec §7.5, SEC-070..075).
//
// Every row stores hash = SHA-256(prev_hash || canonical JSON of the row).
// Rows are appended by Append only, under a transaction-scoped advisory lock,
// with gapless sequence numbers, so any edit, deletion, insertion or reorder
// breaks the chain. Details hold keys and parameters, never secrets.
package audit

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/netip"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"alice/internal/platform/clock"
)

// ActorType says who performed an action.
type ActorType string

// Actor types stored in audit_log.actor_type.
const (
	ActorUser   ActorType = "user"
	ActorToken  ActorType = "api_token"
	ActorSystem ActorType = "system"
)

// Result is the outcome of an audited action.
type Result string

// Results stored in audit_log.result.
const (
	Success Result = "success"
	Failure Result = "failure"
	Denied  Result = "denied"
)

// Actor identifies who acted, from where, in which request.
type Actor struct {
	Type      ActorType
	ID        uuid.UUID // uuid.Nil = none
	Name      string
	IP        netip.Addr // zero = none
	RequestID string
}

// SystemActor is an actor for actions Alice or a host CLI performs itself.
func SystemActor(name string) Actor { return Actor{Type: ActorSystem, Name: name} }

// Event is one action to record.
type Event struct {
	Actor      Actor
	Action     string
	ObjectType string
	ObjectID   string
	Result     Result
	Details    map[string]string
}

// Entry is a stored audit row.
type Entry struct {
	Seq        int64
	At         time.Time
	ActorType  ActorType
	ActorID    uuid.UUID // uuid.Nil = none
	ActorName  string
	IP         string // canonical address, "" = none
	Action     string
	ObjectType string
	ObjectID   string
	Result     Result
	Details    map[string]string
	RequestID  string
	PrevHash   []byte
	Hash       []byte
}

// ErrForbiddenDetailKey is returned when a details key names something that may carry a secret.
var ErrForbiddenDetailKey = errors.New("audit: details key may carry a secret")

var forbiddenKeys = map[string]bool{
	"password": true, "secret": true, "passphrase": true, "token": true, "code": true,
	"totp_secret": true, "private_key": true, "recovery_code": true,
}

// lockKey is the pg_advisory_xact_lock key that serializes chain writes ("caudit" as bytes).
const lockKey int64 = 7160927315633811300

const timeLayout = "2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z07:00"

// Log appends audit events using an injected clock.
type Log struct {
	clock clock.Clock
}

// New returns a Log.
func New(c clock.Clock) *Log { return &Log{clock: c} }

// Append writes e as the next chain link inside tx. The caller's business
// change and its audit row therefore commit or roll back together.
func (l *Log) Append(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, e Event) (Entry, error) {
	if err := checkDetails(e.Details); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, err
	}
	if e.Action == "" || e.Result == "" || e.Actor.Type == "" {
		return Entry{}, errors.New("audit: event needs an actor type, an action and a result")
	}
	if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock($1)", lockKey); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: lock chain: %w", err)
	}
	var lastSeq int64
	prev := make([]byte, sha256.Size)
	err := tx.QueryRow(ctx, "SELECT seq, hash FROM audit_log ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT 1").Scan(&lastSeq, &prev)
	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: read chain head: %w", err)
	}

	en := Entry{
		Seq:        lastSeq + 1,
		At:         l.clock.Now().UTC().Truncate(time.Microsecond),
		ActorType:  e.Actor.Type,
		ActorID:    e.Actor.ID,
		ActorName:  e.Actor.Name,
		IP:         canonicalIP(e.Actor.IP),
		Action:     e.Action,
		ObjectType: e.ObjectType,
		ObjectID:   e.ObjectID,
		Result:     e.Result,
		Details:    copyDetails(e.Details),
		RequestID:  e.Actor.RequestID,
		PrevHash:   prev,
	}
	en.Hash = ComputeHash(prev, en)

	details, err := json.Marshal(en.Details)
	if err != nil {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: encode details: %w", err)
	}
	var actorID *uuid.UUID
	if en.ActorID != uuid.Nil {
		actorID = &en.ActorID
	}
	var ip *string
	if en.IP != "" {
		ip = &en.IP
	}
	_, err = tx.Exec(ctx, `INSERT INTO audit_log
		(seq, at, actor_type, actor_id, actor_name, ip, action, object_type, object_id, result, details, request_id, prev_hash, hash)
		VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6::inet, $7, $8, $9, $10, $11, $12, $13, $14)`,
		en.Seq, en.At, string(en.ActorType), actorID, en.ActorName, ip, en.Action, en.ObjectType, en.ObjectID,
		string(en.Result), details, en.RequestID, en.PrevHash, en.Hash)
	if err != nil {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: insert: %w", err)
	}
	return en, nil
}

type canonical struct {
	Seq        int64             `json:"seq"`
	At         string            `json:"at"`
	ActorType  string            `json:"actor_type"`
	ActorID    string            `json:"actor_id"`
	ActorName  string            `json:"actor_name"`
	IP         string            `json:"ip"`
	Action     string            `json:"action"`
	ObjectType string            `json:"object_type"`
	ObjectID   string            `json:"object_id"`
	Result     string            `json:"result"`
	Details    map[string]string `json:"details"`
	RequestID  string            `json:"request_id"`
}

// ComputeHash returns SHA-256(prev || canonical JSON of e). Field order is
// fixed by the struct, map keys are sorted by encoding/json, the time is
// microsecond-precision UTC, so the hash is identical after a database round trip.
func ComputeHash(prev []byte, e Entry) []byte {
	c := canonical{
		Seq:        e.Seq,
		At:         e.At.UTC().Truncate(time.Microsecond).Format(timeLayout),
		ActorType:  string(e.ActorType),
		ActorName:  e.ActorName,
		IP:         e.IP,
		Action:     e.Action,
		ObjectType: e.ObjectType,
		ObjectID:   e.ObjectID,
		Result:     string(e.Result),
		Details:    copyDetails(e.Details),
		RequestID:  e.RequestID,
	}
	if e.ActorID != uuid.Nil {
		c.ActorID = e.ActorID.String()
	}
	b, err := json.Marshal(c)
	if err != nil {
		panic("audit: canonical encoding failed: " + err.Error()) // only strings and ints: cannot fail
	}
	h := sha256.New()
	h.Write(prev)
	h.Write(b)
	return h.Sum(nil)
}

func checkDetails(d map[string]string) error {
	for k := range d {
		if forbiddenKeys[k] || strings.HasPrefix(k, "private") {
			return fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrForbiddenDetailKey, k)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func copyDetails(d map[string]string) map[string]string {
	out := make(map[string]string, len(d))
	for k, v := range d {
		out[k] = v
	}
	return out
}

func canonicalIP(a netip.Addr) string {
	if !a.IsValid() {
		return ""
	}
	return a.Unmap().String()
}

// canonicalIPText canonicalizes an address read back with host(ip).
func canonicalIPText(s *string) string {
	if s == nil {
		return ""
	}
	a, err := netip.ParseAddr(*s)
	if err != nil {
		return *s
	}
	return a.Unmap().String()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement List and Iterate**

`internal/audit/query.go`:
```go
package audit

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/google/uuid"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"alice/internal/platform/db"
)

// Filter selects entries for List.
type Filter struct {
	Action    string // prefix match, "" = any
	Actor     string // exact actor_name (case-insensitive), "" = any
	Result    Result // "" = any
	BeforeSeq int64  // 0 = newest
	Limit     int    // default 50, max 200
}

const (
	defaultLimit = 50
	maxLimit     = 200
)

const selectColumns = `SELECT seq, at, actor_type, actor_id, actor_name, host(ip), action, object_type, object_id,
	result, details, request_id, prev_hash, hash FROM audit_log`

// List returns entries newest first. next is the seq to pass as BeforeSeq for
// the following page, or 0 when there are no more entries.
func List(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, f Filter) ([]Entry, int64, error) {
	limit := f.Limit
	if limit <= 0 {
		limit = defaultLimit
	}
	limit = min(limit, maxLimit)
	var action, actor, result *string
	if f.Action != "" {
		p := escapeLike(f.Action) + "%"
		action = &p
	}
	if f.Actor != "" {
		actor = &f.Actor
	}
	if f.Result != "" {
		r := string(f.Result)
		result = &r
	}
	var before *int64
	if f.BeforeSeq > 0 {
		before = &f.BeforeSeq
	}
	rows, err := q.Query(ctx, selectColumns+`
		WHERE ($1::text IS NULL OR action LIKE $1 ESCAPE '\')
		  AND ($2::text IS NULL OR lower(actor_name) = lower($2))
		  AND ($3::text IS NULL OR result = $3)
		  AND ($4::bigint IS NULL OR seq < $4)
		ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT $5`, action, actor, result, before, limit+1)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("audit: list: %w", err)
	}
	items, err := collect(rows)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, 0, err
	}
	var next int64
	if len(items) > limit {
		items = items[:limit]
		next = items[limit-1].Seq
	}
	return items, next, nil
}

// Iterate calls fn for every entry in ascending seq order, streaming rows.
func Iterate(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier, fn func(Entry) error) error {
	rows, err := q.Query(ctx, selectColumns+" ORDER BY seq")
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("audit: iterate: %w", err)
	}
	defer rows.Close()
	for rows.Next() {
		e, err := scan(rows)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := fn(e); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return rows.Err()
}

func collect(rows pgx.Rows) ([]Entry, error) {
	defer rows.Close()
	var out []Entry
	for rows.Next() {
		e, err := scan(rows)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		out = append(out, e)
	}
	return out, rows.Err()
}

func scan(rows pgx.Rows) (Entry, error) {
	var (
		e                 Entry
		actorType, result string
		actorID           *uuid.UUID
		ip                *string
		details           []byte
	)
	if err := rows.Scan(&e.Seq, &e.At, &actorType, &actorID, &e.ActorName, &ip, &e.Action, &e.ObjectType,
		&e.ObjectID, &result, &details, &e.RequestID, &e.PrevHash, &e.Hash); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: scan: %w", err)
	}
	e.At = e.At.UTC()
	e.ActorType, e.Result = ActorType(actorType), Result(result)
	if actorID != nil {
		e.ActorID = *actorID
	}
	e.IP = canonicalIPText(ip)
	e.Details = map[string]string{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(details, &e.Details); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: decode details of entry %d: %w", e.Seq, err)
	}
	return e, nil
}

func escapeLike(s string) string {
	return strings.NewReplacer(`\`, `\\`, `%`, `\%`, `_`, `\_`).Replace(s)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement Verify, the checkpoint and the Service**

`internal/audit/verify.go`:
```go
package audit

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strconv"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"

	"alice/internal/platform/db"
	"alice/internal/platform/settings"
)

// Problems reported by Verify.
const (
	ProblemHashMismatch  = "hash_mismatch"
	ProblemSequenceGap   = "sequence_gap"
	ProblemTailTruncated = "tail_truncated"
)

// checkpointKey is the settings key holding the last verified chain head.
const checkpointKey = "audit.checkpoint"

// Report is the result of a chain verification.
type Report struct {
	Intact      bool
	Checked     int64
	HeadSeq     int64
	HeadHash    []byte
	FirstBadSeq int64  // 0 when intact
	Problem     string // "" when intact
}

type checkpoint struct {
	Seq  int64  `json:"seq"`
	Hash string `json:"hash"`
	At   string `json:"at"`
}

// Verify recomputes the whole chain from seq 1 and compares the head with the
// stored checkpoint, so deleting the newest rows is detected too (F-92).
func Verify(ctx context.Context, q db.Querier) (Report, error) {
	var cp checkpoint
	hasCP, err := settings.Get(ctx, q, checkpointKey, &cp)
	if err != nil {
		return Report{}, fmt.Errorf("audit: read checkpoint: %w", err)
	}
	rep := Report{Intact: true}
	prev := make([]byte, sha256.Size)
	errStop := errors.New("stop")
	err = Iterate(ctx, q, func(e Entry) error {
		expected := rep.HeadSeq + 1
		if e.Seq != expected {
			rep.fail(expected, ProblemSequenceGap)
			return errStop
		}
		if !bytes.Equal(e.PrevHash, prev) || !bytes.Equal(ComputeHash(prev, e), e.Hash) {
			rep.fail(e.Seq, ProblemHashMismatch)
			return errStop
		}
		if hasCP && e.Seq == cp.Seq && hex.EncodeToString(e.Hash) != cp.Hash {
			rep.fail(e.Seq, ProblemHashMismatch)
			return errStop
		}
		rep.Checked++
		rep.HeadSeq, rep.HeadHash, prev = e.Seq, e.Hash, e.Hash
		return nil
	})
	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, errStop) {
		return Report{}, err
	}
	if rep.Intact && hasCP && cp.Seq > rep.HeadSeq {
		rep.fail(rep.HeadSeq+1, ProblemTailTruncated)
	}
	return rep, nil
}

func (r *Report) fail(seq int64, problem string) {
	r.Intact, r.FirstBadSeq, r.Problem = false, seq, problem
}

// Service binds the log to a pool for callers outside a transaction (the API and CLI).
type Service struct {
	log  *Log
	pool *pgxpool.Pool
}

// NewService returns a Service.
func NewService(l *Log, pool *pgxpool.Pool) *Service { return &Service{log: l, pool: pool} }

// Write appends e in its own transaction.
func (s *Service) Write(ctx context.Context, e Event) error {
	return db.InTx(ctx, s.pool, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		_, err := s.log.Append(ctx, tx, e)
		return err
	})
}

// List pages entries newest first.
func (s *Service) List(ctx context.Context, f Filter) ([]Entry, int64, error) {
	return List(ctx, s.pool, f)
}

// Iterate streams entries in ascending order.
func (s *Service) Iterate(ctx context.Context, fn func(Entry) error) error {
	return Iterate(ctx, s.pool, fn)
}

// Verify checks the chain, stores a new checkpoint when it is intact, and
// records "audit.verified" (success, or failure with the problem).
func (s *Service) Verify(ctx context.Context, actor Actor) (Report, error) {
	rep, err := Verify(ctx, s.pool)
	if err != nil {
		return Report{}, err
	}
	ev := Event{Actor: actor, Action: "audit.verified", ObjectType: "audit_log", Result: Success,
		Details: map[string]string{"checked": strconv.FormatInt(rep.Checked, 10), "head_seq": strconv.FormatInt(rep.HeadSeq, 10)}}
	if !rep.Intact {
		ev.Result = Failure
		ev.Details["problem"] = rep.Problem
		ev.Details["first_bad_seq"] = strconv.FormatInt(rep.FirstBadSeq, 10)
	}
	err = db.InTx(ctx, s.pool, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		if rep.Intact && rep.HeadSeq > 0 {
			cp := checkpoint{Seq: rep.HeadSeq, Hash: hex.EncodeToString(rep.HeadHash),
				At: s.log.clock.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)}
			if err := settings.Set(ctx, tx, checkpointKey, cp, s.log.clock.Now()); err != nil {
				return fmt.Errorf("audit: store checkpoint: %w", err)
			}
		}
		_, err := s.log.Append(ctx, tx, ev)
		return err
	})
	return rep, err
}
```

### Task 7: RBAC: roles, scopes, permissions and decisions

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/rbac/rbac.go`
- Test: `internal/rbac/rbac_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing (pure package; `internal/archtest` already allows `alice/internal/rbac/...` → platform only, and this package imports nothing internal).
- Produces (exact):
  - `type Role string`; `const Admin Role = "admin"`, `Operator Role = "operator"`, `Auditor Role = "auditor"`; `func Roles() []Role` (admin, operator, auditor); `func ParseRole(s string) (Role, bool)` (exact lowercase only); `func (r Role) AllowsScope(s Scope) bool`.
  - `type Scope string`; `const ScopeRead Scope = "read"`, `ScopeOperate Scope = "operate"`; `func ParseScope(s string) (Scope, bool)`.
  - `type Permission string` with constants `Public`, `Setup`, `MFAPending`, `Session`, `SessionStepUp`, `Read`, `AuditRead`, `UsersRead`, `UsersManage`, `SettingsManage`, `SetupFinish` (values `public`, `setup`, `mfa_pending`, `session`, `session.stepup`, `read`, `audit.read`, `users.read`, `users.manage`, `settings.manage`, `setup.finish`).
  - `type Policy struct { Anonymous, Setup, Pending bool; Roles []Role; TokenScope Scope; StepUp bool }`; `func PolicyFor(p Permission) (Policy, bool)` (Roles is a copy); `func Permissions() []Permission` (sorted).
  - `type SubjectKind int` with `Anonymous`, `SetupSession`, `PendingSession`, `UserSession`, `APIToken`; `type Subject struct { Kind SubjectKind; Role Role; Scope Scope; StepUp bool }`.
  - `type Decision int` with `Allow`, `DenyUnauthenticated`, `DenyForbidden`, `DenyStepUp`; `func Decide(p Permission, s Subject) Decision`.
  - Used by Task 10 (`Principal.Subject`), Task 11 (token scopes), Task 13 (route table, guard, authz matrix) and the web role checks.

The expected matrix in the test is written by hand from the spec role table (04-security.md §15.4, decisions R3): it is the oracle and must never be generated from the policy map.

```go
func TestDecideMatchesDocumentedMatrix(t *testing.T) {
	for _, p := range rbac.Permissions() {
		row, ok := expected[p]
		require.True(t, ok, "permission %q has no row in the expected matrix", p)
		require.Len(t, row, len(subjects), "row %q must cover every subject", p)
		for name, s := range subjects {
			want, ok := row[name]
			require.True(t, ok, "row %q misses subject %q", p, name)
			assert.Equal(t, want, rbac.Decide(p, s), fmt.Sprintf("%s as %s", p, name))
		}
	}
	assert.Len(t, expected, len(rbac.Permissions()), "expected matrix lists a permission that does not exist")
}

func TestUnknownPermissionIsDenied(t *testing.T) {
	for name, s := range subjects {
		assert.Equal(t, rbac.DenyForbidden, rbac.Decide("nope", s), name)
		assert.Equal(t, rbac.DenyForbidden, rbac.Decide("", s), name)
	}
}

func TestPermissionsAreSortedAndHavePolicies(t *testing.T) {
	ps := rbac.Permissions()
	assert.Len(t, ps, 11)
	assert.True(t, sort.SliceIsSorted(ps, func(i, j int) bool { return ps[i] < ps[j] }))
	for _, p := range ps {
		_, ok := rbac.PolicyFor(p)
		assert.True(t, ok, string(p))
	}
	_, ok := rbac.PolicyFor("nope")
	assert.False(t, ok)
}

func TestPolicyForReturnsACopy(t *testing.T) {
	p, ok := rbac.PolicyFor(rbac.Read)
	require.True(t, ok)
	p.Roles[0] = "root"
	again, _ := rbac.PolicyFor(rbac.Read)
	assert.Equal(t, rbac.Admin, again.Roles[0])
}

func TestRolesAndParsing(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Equal(t, []rbac.Role{rbac.Admin, rbac.Operator, rbac.Auditor}, rbac.Roles())
	for _, r := range rbac.Roles() {
		got, ok := rbac.ParseRole(string(r))
		assert.True(t, ok)
		assert.Equal(t, r, got)
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{"", "Admin", "ADMIN", "root", " admin"} {
		_, ok := rbac.ParseRole(bad)
		assert.False(t, ok, bad)
	}
	for _, s := range []rbac.Scope{rbac.ScopeRead, rbac.ScopeOperate} {
		got, ok := rbac.ParseScope(string(s))
		assert.True(t, ok)
		assert.Equal(t, s, got)
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{"", "Read", "write", "admin"} {
		_, ok := rbac.ParseScope(bad)
		assert.False(t, ok, bad)
	}
}

func TestAllowsScope(t *testing.T) {
	assert.True(t, rbac.Admin.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeRead))
	assert.True(t, rbac.Admin.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeOperate))
	assert.True(t, rbac.Operator.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeRead))
	assert.True(t, rbac.Operator.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeOperate))
	assert.True(t, rbac.Auditor.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeRead))
	assert.False(t, rbac.Auditor.AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeOperate), "auditors are read-only")
	assert.False(t, rbac.Admin.AllowsScope("write"))
	assert.False(t, rbac.Role("root").AllowsScope(rbac.ScopeRead))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the package**

`internal/rbac/rbac.go`:
```go
// Package rbac holds Alice's role and permission model. It is pure: no I/O,
// no clock, no database. Every HTTP route declares one Permission; Decide
// turns (permission, subject) into an authorization decision.
package rbac

import "sort"

// Role is a user's role. Roles are stored lowercase in the database.
type Role string

// The three v1 roles (spec §15.4).
const (
	Admin    Role = "admin"
	Operator Role = "operator"
	Auditor  Role = "auditor"
)

// Roles returns every role, most privileged first.
func Roles() []Role { return []Role{Admin, Operator, Auditor} }

// ParseRole accepts only the exact lowercase role names.
func ParseRole(s string) (Role, bool) {
	for _, r := range Roles() {
		if string(r) == s {
			return r, true
		}
	}
	return "", false
}

// Scope is an API token scope. ScopeOperate includes everything ScopeRead allows.
type Scope string

// API token scopes (SEC-026).
const (
	ScopeRead    Scope = "read"
	ScopeOperate Scope = "operate"
)

// ParseScope accepts only the exact lowercase scope names.
func ParseScope(s string) (Scope, bool) {
	switch Scope(s) {
	case ScopeRead, ScopeOperate:
		return Scope(s), true
	default:
		return "", false
	}
}

// AllowsScope reports whether a user with role r may hold a token with scope s.
// Auditors are read-only; admins and operators may hold either scope.
func (r Role) AllowsScope(s Scope) bool {
	switch r {
	case Admin, Operator:
		return s == ScopeRead || s == ScopeOperate
	case Auditor:
		return s == ScopeRead
	default:
		return false
	}
}

// satisfies reports whether a token with scope have meets the minimum scope want.
func (have Scope) satisfies(want Scope) bool {
	switch want {
	case ScopeRead:
		return have == ScopeRead || have == ScopeOperate
	case ScopeOperate:
		return have == ScopeOperate
	default:
		return false
	}
}

// Permission is the value of a route's x-permission.
type Permission string

// Every permission used by the Phase 1 route table.
const (
	Public         Permission = "public"
	Setup          Permission = "setup"
	MFAPending     Permission = "mfa_pending"
	Session        Permission = "session"
	SessionStepUp  Permission = "session.stepup"
	Read           Permission = "read"
	AuditRead      Permission = "audit.read"
	UsersRead      Permission = "users.read"
	UsersManage    Permission = "users.manage"
	SettingsManage Permission = "settings.manage"
	SetupFinish    Permission = "setup.finish"
)

// Policy describes who may use a permission.
type Policy struct {
	Anonymous  bool   // anyone, authenticated or not
	Setup      bool   // setup (wizard) session only
	Pending    bool   // pending login session only (password verified, MFA not done)
	Roles      []Role // roles allowed for user sessions and API tokens
	TokenScope Scope  // "" = API tokens rejected; otherwise the minimum scope (operate ⊇ read)
	StepUp     bool   // user sessions must have an active step-up window
}

var allRoles = []Role{Admin, Operator, Auditor}

var policies = map[Permission]Policy{
	Public:         {Anonymous: true},
	Setup:          {Setup: true},
	MFAPending:     {Pending: true},
	Session:        {Roles: allRoles},
	SessionStepUp:  {Roles: allRoles, StepUp: true},
	Read:           {Roles: allRoles, TokenScope: ScopeRead},
	AuditRead:      {Roles: []Role{Admin, Auditor}, TokenScope: ScopeRead},
	UsersRead:      {Roles: []Role{Admin}, TokenScope: ScopeRead},
	UsersManage:    {Roles: []Role{Admin}, StepUp: true},
	SettingsManage: {Roles: []Role{Admin}, StepUp: true},
	SetupFinish:    {Roles: []Role{Admin}},
}

// PolicyFor returns the policy of p. The returned Roles slice is a copy.
func PolicyFor(p Permission) (Policy, bool) {
	pol, ok := policies[p]
	if !ok {
		return Policy{}, false
	}
	pol.Roles = append([]Role(nil), pol.Roles...)
	return pol, true
}

// Permissions returns every known permission, sorted.
func Permissions() []Permission {
	out := make([]Permission, 0, len(policies))
	for p := range policies {
		out = append(out, p)
	}
	sort.Slice(out, func(i, j int) bool { return out[i] < out[j] })
	return out
}

// SubjectKind is how the caller authenticated.
type SubjectKind int

// Subject kinds, from least to most authenticated.
const (
	Anonymous SubjectKind = iota
	SetupSession
	PendingSession
	UserSession
	APIToken
)

// Subject is the caller as seen by the authorization check.
type Subject struct {
	Kind   SubjectKind
	Role   Role
	Scope  Scope // API tokens only
	StepUp bool  // user sessions only: step-up window is active
}

// Decision is the outcome of Decide.
type Decision int

// Decisions. DenyUnauthenticated maps to HTTP 401, the others to 403.
const (
	Allow Decision = iota
	DenyUnauthenticated
	DenyForbidden
	DenyStepUp
)

// Decide is the single authorization function (deny by default).
func Decide(p Permission, s Subject) Decision {
	pol, ok := policies[p]
	switch {
	case !ok:
		return DenyForbidden
	case pol.Anonymous:
		return Allow
	case pol.Setup:
		return exact(s.Kind, SetupSession)
	case pol.Pending:
		return exact(s.Kind, PendingSession)
	}
	switch s.Kind {
	case UserSession:
		if !hasRole(pol.Roles, s.Role) {
			return DenyForbidden
		}
		if pol.StepUp && !s.StepUp {
			return DenyStepUp
		}
		return Allow
	case APIToken:
		if pol.TokenScope == "" || !s.Scope.satisfies(pol.TokenScope) || !hasRole(pol.Roles, s.Role) {
			return DenyForbidden
		}
		return Allow
	default: // Anonymous, SetupSession, PendingSession are not fully authenticated users
		return DenyUnauthenticated
	}
}

func exact(have, want SubjectKind) Decision {
	switch have {
	case want:
		return Allow
	case Anonymous:
		return DenyUnauthenticated
	default:
		return DenyForbidden
	}
}

func hasRole(roles []Role, r Role) bool {
	for _, x := range roles {
		if x == r {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}
```

### Task 8: Passwords: Argon2id hasher, policy and breached-password list

**Files:**
- Create: `tools/breachlist/main.go`, `tools/breachlist/main_test.go`
- Create: `internal/auth/breached/breached.go`, `internal/auth/breached/breached_test.go`, `internal/auth/breached/ncsc-100k.bin` (generated, committed)
- Create: `internal/auth/password.go`, `internal/auth/password_test.go`
- Create: `internal/auth/policy.go`, `internal/auth/policy_test.go`
- Modify: `go.mod`, `go.sum` (`golang.org/x/crypto` becomes a direct dependency at v0.57.0)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from earlier Phase 1 tasks.
- Produces (exact, package `alice/internal/auth`):
  - `type Argon2Params struct { MemoryKiB uint32; Time uint32; Threads uint8; SaltLen uint32; KeyLen uint32 }`
  - `var PasswordParams = Argon2Params{MemoryKiB: 64 * 1024, Time: 3, Threads: 2, SaltLen: 16, KeyLen: 32}`
  - `var RecoveryCodeParams = Argon2Params{MemoryKiB: 8 * 1024, Time: 1, Threads: 1, SaltLen: 16, KeyLen: 32}`
  - `type Hasher struct{...}`; `func NewHasher(p Argon2Params, maxConcurrent int) *Hasher`; `func (h *Hasher) Hash(ctx context.Context, secret string) (string, error)` → PHC `$argon2id$v=19$m=65536,t=3,p=2$<salt>$<key>` (unpadded std base64); `func (h *Hasher) Verify(ctx context.Context, encoded, secret string) (ok bool, needsRehash bool, err error)`; `func (h *Hasher) DummyVerify(ctx context.Context, secret string)`.
  - `const MinPasswordLength = 12`, `MaxPasswordLength = 128` (runes after NFKC); `func NormalizePassword(pw string) string`; `func CheckPasswordPolicy(pw string) error`; `var ErrPasswordTooShort, ErrPasswordTooLong, ErrPasswordBreached error`.
  - Package `alice/internal/auth/breached`: `func Contains(password string) bool`, `func Len() int`.
- Callers hash `NormalizePassword(pw)` and verify `NormalizePassword(pw)` (Tasks 10, 11, 12). `Hasher.Hash`/`Verify` take a context because they wait on the concurrency semaphore.

Dependency note: `golang.org/x/crypto` is in the spec's runtime budget (§5.2) and required by SEC-001 (Argon2id). No other new dependency.

The breached list (SEC-003) is the NCSC top 100,000 from SecLists, pinned to commit `1a7bb9127eca9e6ff2fc0301c597fe6e16a0cb56` (MIT licence). Only 8-byte SHA-256 prefixes are committed (99,839 unique entries, 798,712 bytes); with 2^64 possible prefixes the false-positive rate is negligible and the plaintext list never enters the repository (nothing for gitleaks to flag).

```go
package main

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/binary"
	"os"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

func prefix(s string) uint64 {
	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(s))
	return binary.BigEndian.Uint64(sum[:8])
}

func TestHashesAreSortedUniqueAndSkipBlankLines(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := hashes(strings.NewReader("banana\r\napple\n\napple\ncherry"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, got, 3)
	assert.IsIncreasing(t, got)
	assert.Contains(t, got, prefix("banana"), "trailing CR is stripped")
	assert.Contains(t, got, prefix("apple"))
	assert.Contains(t, got, prefix("cherry"))
}

func TestRunRejectsUnexpectedSource(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	in := dir + "/list.txt"
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(in, []byte("password\n"), 0o600))
	_, err := run(in, dir+"/out.bin")
	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "wrong or modified file")
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the generator**

`tools/breachlist/main.go`:
```go
// Command breachlist converts the pinned NCSC top-100k password list into the
// compact lookup file embedded by internal/auth/breached: sorted, de-duplicated
// big-endian uint64 values, each the first 8 bytes of SHA-256(password).
//
// Usage: go run ./tools/breachlist -in <list.txt> -out internal/auth/breached/ncsc-100k.bin
package main

import (
	"bufio"
	"bytes"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/binary"
	"encoding/hex"
	"flag"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"os"
	"slices"
)

// sourceSHA256 pins SecLists commit 1a7bb9127eca9e6ff2fc0301c597fe6e16a0cb56,
// Passwords/Common-Credentials/100k-most-used-passwords-NCSC.txt (MIT licence).
const sourceSHA256 = "c2e5696882c603b76bb67a47ee970897e5a76fc4c3f5547abe3d0ca340c576e0"

func main() {
	in := flag.String("in", "", "path of the downloaded password list")
	out := flag.String("out", "", "path of the .bin file to write")
	flag.Parse()
	if *in == "" || *out == "" {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "usage: breachlist -in <list.txt> -out <file.bin>")
		os.Exit(2)
	}
	n, err := run(*in, *out)
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "breachlist:", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	fmt.Fprintf(os.Stdout, "wrote %d entries to %s\n", n, *out)
}

func run(inPath, outPath string) (int, error) {
	data, err := os.ReadFile(inPath) //nolint:gosec // G304: developer tool, path from the command line
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	sum := sha256.Sum256(data)
	if got := hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]); got != sourceSHA256 {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("source list has sha256 %s, want %s (wrong or modified file)", got, sourceSHA256)
	}
	values, err := hashes(bytes.NewReader(data))
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	buf := make([]byte, 8*len(values))
	for i, v := range values {
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint64(buf[8*i:], v)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(outPath, buf, 0o644); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // G306: public, non-secret data file
		return 0, err
	}
	return len(values), nil
}

// hashes returns the sorted, de-duplicated 8-byte SHA-256 prefixes of every
// non-empty line. Lines are used exactly as written (a trailing CR is removed).
func hashes(r io.Reader) ([]uint64, error) {
	var out []uint64
	sc := bufio.NewScanner(r)
	for sc.Scan() {
		line := bytes.TrimSuffix(sc.Bytes(), []byte("\r"))
		if len(line) == 0 {
			continue
		}
		sum := sha256.Sum256(line)
		out = append(out, binary.BigEndian.Uint64(sum[:8]))
	}
	if err := sc.Err(); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	slices.Sort(out)
	return slices.Compact(out), nil
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement the lookup**

`internal/auth/breached/breached.go`:
```go
// Package breached answers "is this password on the list of the 100,000 most
// used passwords?" fully offline (SEC-003). The list is the NCSC top-100k from
// SecLists, stored as sorted 8-byte SHA-256 prefixes; regenerate it with
// tools/breachlist.
package breached

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	_ "embed"
	"encoding/binary"
	"sort"
)

//go:embed ncsc-100k.bin
var list []byte

// Len returns the number of entries in the embedded list.
func Len() int { return len(list) / 8 }

func at(i int) uint64 { return binary.BigEndian.Uint64(list[8*i : 8*i+8]) }

// Contains reports whether password (compared exactly, byte for byte) is on the list.
func Contains(password string) bool {
	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(password))
	want := binary.BigEndian.Uint64(sum[:8])
	n := Len()
	i := sort.Search(n, func(i int) bool { return at(i) >= want })
	return i < n && at(i) == want
}
```

```go
// testParams keep unit tests fast; production parameters are asserted separately.
var testParams = Argon2Params{MemoryKiB: 64, Time: 1, Threads: 1, SaltLen: 16, KeyLen: 32}

func TestProductionParametersMatchSpec(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Equal(t, Argon2Params{MemoryKiB: 65536, Time: 3, Threads: 2, SaltLen: 16, KeyLen: 32}, PasswordParams)
	enc, err := NewHasher(PasswordParams, 1).Hash(context.Background(), "correct horse battery staple")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, strings.HasPrefix(enc, "$argon2id$v=19$m=65536,t=3,p=2$"), enc)
	parts := strings.Split(enc, "$")
	require.Len(t, parts, 6)
	assert.Len(t, parts[4], 22, "16-byte salt, unpadded base64")
	assert.Len(t, parts[5], 43, "32-byte key, unpadded base64")

func TestHashAndVerify(t *testing.T) {
	ctx := context.Background()
	h := NewHasher(testParams, 2)
	enc, err := h.Hash(ctx, "İzmir-ığdır-2026")
	require.NoError(t, err)

	ok, rehash, err := h.Verify(ctx, enc, "İzmir-ığdır-2026")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.False(t, rehash)

	ok, _, err = h.Verify(ctx, enc, "izmir-ığdır-2026")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, ok)

	enc2, err := h.Hash(ctx, "İzmir-ığdır-2026")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.NotEqual(t, enc, enc2, "every hash uses a fresh salt")
}

func TestVerifyFlagsWeakerParametersForRehash(t *testing.T) {
	ctx := context.Background()
	weak, err := NewHasher(testParams, 1).Hash(ctx, "secret-password-1")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	stronger := NewHasher(Argon2Params{MemoryKiB: 128, Time: 2, Threads: 1, SaltLen: 16, KeyLen: 32}, 1)
	ok, rehash, err := stronger.Verify(ctx, weak, "secret-password-1")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, ok, "old hashes keep working")
	assert.True(t, rehash)
}

	h := NewHasher(testParams, 1)
	for _, enc := range []string{
```
