```ts
export type Copy = (typeof COPY)[Lang];

export function browserLocale(lang: Lang): string {
  return lang === 'tr' ? 'tr-TR' : 'en-US';
}

function escapeRegExp(s: string): string {
  return s.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
}

// field finds an input by its exact label; Mantine appends " *" to required labels.
export function field(scope: Page | Locator, label: string): Locator {
  return scope.getByLabel(new RegExp(`^${escapeRegExp(label)}( \\*)?$`));
}

// either matches a copy string in English or Turkish (substring), for screens reached before the
// language is known or after a user's own language setting may have switched it.
export function either(key: 'loginHeading' | 'noAccess' | 'sessionEnded' | 'wizardHeading'): RegExp {
  return new RegExp(`${escapeRegExp(COPY.en[key])}|${escapeRegExp(COPY.tr[key])}`);
}

// uiLang reads the language the app is currently rendered in (<html lang>).
export async function uiLang(page: Page): Promise<Lang> {
  return (await page.locator('html').getAttribute('lang')) === 'tr' ? 'tr' : 'en';
}

export async function resetInstall(request: APIRequestContext): Promise<void> {
  const res = await request.post('/test/reset');
  expect(res.status(), 'POST /test/reset (is this the e2e build? run make e2e-up)').toBe(204);
}

export async function serverNow(request: APIRequestContext): Promise<number> {
  const res = await request.get('/test/clock');
  expect(res.ok()).toBe(true);
  const { now } = (await res.json()) as { now: string };
  return Date.parse(now.replace(/(\.\d{3})\d+/, '$1'));
}

export async function advanceClock(request: APIRequestContext, seconds: number): Promise<void> {
  const res = await request.post('/test/clock', { data: { advance_seconds: seconds } });
  expect(res.ok()).toBe(true);
}

export async function setupCode(request: APIRequestContext): Promise<string> {
  const res = await request.post('/test/setup-token');
  expect(res.ok()).toBe(true);
  return ((await res.json()) as { code: string }).code;
}

function base32Decode(input: string): Buffer {
  const alphabet = 'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ234567';
  let bits = 0;
  let value = 0;
  const out: number[] = [];
  for (const ch of input.replace(/[\s=]/g, '')) {
    const idx = alphabet.indexOf(ch);
    if (idx < 0) throw new Error(`invalid base32 character ${ch}`);
    value = (value << 5) | idx;
    bits += 5;
    if (bits >= 8) {
      out.push((value >>> (bits - 8)) & 0xff);
      bits -= 8;
    }
  }
  return Buffer.from(out);
}

// totp computes the RFC 6238 code (SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s) for the given instant.
export function totp(secretBase32: string, atMs: number): string {
  const counter = Buffer.alloc(8);
  counter.writeBigUInt64BE(BigInt(Math.floor(atMs / 1000 / 30)));
  const mac = createHmac('sha1', base32Decode(secretBase32)).update(counter).digest();
  const offset = mac[mac.length - 1] & 0x0f;
  const bin = ((mac[offset] & 0x7f) << 24) | (mac[offset + 1] << 16) | (mac[offset + 2] << 8) | mac[offset + 3];
  return String(bin % 1_000_000).padStart(6, '0');
}

// freshCode moves the server clock past the last used 30 s step, so a code is never replayed,
// and returns the code for the server's current time.
export async function freshCode(request: APIRequestContext, secret: string): Promise<string> {
  await advanceClock(request, 31);
  return totp(secret, await serverNow(request));
}

// enrollTotp completes the "Set up your authenticator app" and recovery-code screens.
export async function enrollTotp(page: Page, request: APIRequestContext, c: Copy): Promise<{ totpSecret: string; recoveryCodes: string[] }> {
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.totpHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await expect(page.locator('img[src^="data:image/png;base64,"]')).toBeVisible();
  const totpSecret = ((await page.getByTestId('totp-secret').textContent()) ?? '').replace(/\s/g, '');
  expect(totpSecret).toMatch(/^[A-Z2-7]{32}$/);
  await field(page, c.code).fill(totp(totpSecret, await serverNow(request)));
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verify }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.recoveryHeading })).toBeVisible();
  const text = (await page.getByTestId('recovery-codes').textContent()) ?? '';
  const recoveryCodes = text.match(/[0-9A-Z]{4}-[0-9A-Z]{4}-[0-9A-Z]{4}-[0-9A-Z]{4}/g) ?? [];
  expect(recoveryCodes).toHaveLength(10);
  await page.getByLabel(c.savedCodes).check();
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.continue }).click();
  return { totpSecret, recoveryCodes };
}

export interface Account {
  username: string;
  fullName: string;
  password: string;
}

export const ADMIN: Account = { username: 'admin', fullName: 'Ada Yılmaz', password: 'correct-horse-battery-2026' };
export const PASSPHRASE = 'kırmızı-kedi-mavi-deniz-2026';

// runWizard installs Alice from scratch through the setup wizard (Phase 1 steps).
export async function runWizard(
  page: Page,
  request: APIRequestContext,
  lang: Lang,
  admin: Account = ADMIN,
): Promise<{ totpSecret: string; recoveryCodes: string[] }> {
  const c = COPY[lang];
  const code = await setupCode(request);
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/setup$/);
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.wizardHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await field(page, c.setupCode).fill(code);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.continue }).click();

  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.languageHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.continue }).click(); // keeps the preselected (current) language

  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.adminHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await field(page, c.username).fill(admin.username);
  await field(page, c.fullName).fill(admin.fullName);
  await field(page, c.password).fill('too-short11');
  await field(page, c.repeatPassword).fill('too-short11');
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.continue }).click();
  // Alice refuses a password shorter than 12 characters and says why (the minimum is shown).
  await expect(page.getByRole('alert').or(page.locator('input[aria-invalid="true"]')).first()).toBeVisible();
  await expect(page.getByText(/\b12\b/).first()).toBeVisible();
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.totpHeading })).toHaveCount(0);

  await field(page, c.password).fill(admin.password);
  await field(page, c.repeatPassword).fill(admin.password);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.continue }).click();
  const enrolled = await enrollTotp(page, request, c);

  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.passphraseHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await field(page, c.passphrase).fill(PASSPHRASE);
  await field(page, c.repeatPassphrase).fill(PASSPHRASE);
  await page.getByLabel(c.storedPassphrase).check();
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.savePassphrase }).click();

  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.doneHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.toDashboard }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.welcome(admin.fullName) })).toBeVisible();
  return enrolled;
}

// submitLogin fills the sign-in form and returns the HTTP status of POST /auth/login.
export async function submitLogin(page: Page, c: Copy, username: string, password: string): Promise<number> {
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.loginHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await field(page, c.username).fill(username);
  await field(page, c.password).fill(password);
  const [res] = await Promise.all([
    page.waitForResponse((r) => r.url().endsWith('/api/v1/auth/login') && r.request().method() === 'POST'),
    page.getByRole('button', { name: c.signIn }).click(),
  ]);
  return res.status();
}

// signIn signs an enrolled user in with password and a fresh TOTP code.
export async function signIn(page: Page, request: APIRequestContext, account: Account, totpSecret: string): Promise<void> {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: either('loginHeading') })).toBeVisible();
  const c = COPY[await uiLang(page)];
  expect(await submitLogin(page, c, account.username, account.password)).toBe(200);
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.mfaHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await field(page, c.code).fill(await freshCode(request, totpSecret));
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verify }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: c.signOut })).toBeVisible();
}

// firstSignIn signs in a user created by an administrator; they enroll TOTP at the first sign-in.
export async function firstSignIn(page: Page, request: APIRequestContext, account: Account): Promise<string> {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: either('loginHeading') })).toBeVisible();
  const c = COPY[await uiLang(page)];
  expect(await submitLogin(page, c, account.username, account.password)).toBe(200);
  const { totpSecret } = await enrollTotp(page, request, c);
  await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: COPY[await uiLang(page)].signOut })).toBeVisible();
  return totpSecret;
}

export async function signOut(page: Page): Promise<void> {
  const c = COPY[await uiLang(page)];
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.signOut }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: either('loginHeading') })).toBeVisible();
}

export async function expectAccessible(page: Page, screen: string): Promise<void> {
  expect(await seriousViolations(page), `axe: ${screen}`).toEqual([]);
}

export async function seriousViolations(page: Page): Promise<string[]> {
  const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page }).analyze();
  return results.violations
    .filter((v) => v.impact === 'serious' || v.impact === 'critical')
    .map((v) => `${v.impact}: ${v.id} (${v.nodes.map((n) => n.target.join(' ')).join(', ')})`);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the journey tests**

`web/e2e/phase1.spec.ts`:
```ts
import { expect, test } from '@playwright/test';
import {
  ADMIN,
  COPY,
  advanceClock,
  browserLocale,
  either,
  expectAccessible,
  field,
  firstSignIn,
  freshCode,
  resetInstall,
  runWizard,
  signIn,
  signOut,
  submitLogin,
  totp,
  serverNow,
  uiLang,
  type Account,
  type Copy,
  type Lang,
} from './helpers';
import type { Page, APIRequestContext } from '@playwright/test';

const OPERATOR: Account = { username: 'operator1', fullName: 'Oğuz Işık', password: 'operator-password-2026' };
const AUDITOR: Account = { username: 'auditor1', fullName: 'Ayşe Denetçi', password: 'auditor-password-2026' };

async function openNav(page: Page, name: string) {
  await page.getByRole('link', { name, exact: true }).click();
}

// createUser adds a user on the Users page; a step-up prompt is answered when it appears.
async function createUser(page: Page, request: APIRequestContext, c: Copy, account: Account, role: 'operator' | 'auditor', totpSecret: string) {
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.addUser }).click();
  const dialog = page.getByRole('dialog', { name: c.addUser });
  await field(dialog, c.username).fill(account.username);
  await field(dialog, c.fullName).fill(account.fullName);
  await field(dialog, c.role).selectOption(role);
  await field(dialog, c.initialPassword).fill(account.password);
  await dialog.getByRole('button', { name: c.createUser }).click();
  const stepUp = page.getByRole('dialog', { name: c.stepUpHeading });
  const row = page.getByRole('row').filter({ hasText: account.username });
  await expect(stepUp.or(row).first()).toBeVisible();
  if (await stepUp.isVisible()) {
    await field(stepUp, c.code).fill(await freshCode(request, totpSecret));
    await stepUp.getByRole('button', { name: c.stepUpConfirm }).click();
  }
  await expect(row).toBeVisible();
}

for (const lang of ['en', 'tr'] as Lang[]) {
  const c = COPY[lang];

  test.describe(`Phase 1 owner journey (${lang})`, () => {
    test.use({ locale: browserLocale(lang) });
    test.beforeEach(async ({ request }) => {
      await resetInstall(request);
    });

    test('wizard creates the admin, TOTP sign-in, wrong code, sign-out and idle timeout', async ({ page, request }) => {
      const { totpSecret } = await runWizard(page, request, lang);
      expect(await uiLang(page)).toBe(lang);
      await signOut(page);

      // Wrong verification code, then the right one.
      await page.goto('/login');
      expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, ADMIN.password)).toBe(200);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.mfaHeading })).toBeVisible();
      const good = await freshCode(request, totpSecret);
      const wrong = String((Number(good) + 500_000) % 1_000_000).padStart(6, '0');
      await field(page, c.code).fill(wrong);
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verify }).click();
      await expect(page.getByText(c.mfaInvalid)).toBeVisible();
      await field(page, c.code).fill(totp(totpSecret, await serverNow(request)));
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verify }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.welcome(ADMIN.fullName) })).toBeVisible();

      // Idle timeout: 31 minutes without activity ends the session.
      await advanceClock(request, 31 * 60);
      await openNav(page, c.navUsers);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: either('loginHeading') })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.getByText(either('sessionEnded'))).toBeVisible();

      // Signing in again works.
      await signIn(page, request, ADMIN, totpSecret);
    });

    test('repeated wrong passwords slow down and then lock the account for 15 minutes', async ({ page, request }) => {
      const { totpSecret } = await runWizard(page, request, lang);
      await signOut(page);
      await page.goto('/login');

      for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
        expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, 'wrong-password-123')).toBe(401);
      }
      await expect(page.getByText(c.invalidCredentials)).toBeVisible();
      for (let i = 6; i <= 9; i++) {
        await advanceClock(request, 61); // wait out the progressive delay
        expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, 'wrong-password-123')).toBe(401);
      }
      // Still inside the 16 s delay after the 9th failure: even the right password is refused.
      expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, ADMIN.password)).toBe(401);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.mfaHeading })).toHaveCount(0);

      await advanceClock(request, 61);
      expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, 'wrong-password-123')).toBe(401); // 10th: locked
      await advanceClock(request, 61);
      expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, ADMIN.password)).toBe(401);
      await expect(page.getByText(c.invalidCredentials)).toBeVisible();

      await advanceClock(request, 15 * 60);
      expect(await submitLogin(page, c, ADMIN.username, ADMIN.password)).toBe(200);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.mfaHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await field(page, c.code).fill(await freshCode(request, totpSecret));
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verify }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.welcome(ADMIN.fullName) })).toBeVisible();
    });

    test('three roles behave differently and the audit chain verifies', async ({ page, request }) => {
      const { totpSecret } = await runWizard(page, request, lang);

      // Let the step-up window from the wizard sign-in expire, so creating a user asks for a code.
      await advanceClock(request, 6 * 60);
      await openNav(page, c.navUsers);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.usersHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await createUser(page, request, c, OPERATOR, 'operator', totpSecret);
      await createUser(page, request, c, AUDITOR, 'auditor', totpSecret);

      await openNav(page, c.navAudit);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.auditHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.getByText(c.userCreatedAction).first()).toBeVisible();
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verifyIntegrity }).click();
      await expect(page.getByText(c.intact)).toBeVisible();
      await signOut(page);

      // Operator: no Users, no Audit log.
      await firstSignIn(page, request, OPERATOR);
      let u = COPY[await uiLang(page)];
      await expect(page.getByRole('link', { name: u.navTokens, exact: true })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.getByRole('link', { name: u.navUsers, exact: true })).toHaveCount(0);
      await expect(page.getByRole('link', { name: u.navAudit, exact: true })).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.goto('/users');
      await expect(page.getByText(either('noAccess'))).toBeVisible();
      await page.goto('/audit');
      await expect(page.getByText(either('noAccess'))).toBeVisible();
      await signOut(page);

      // Auditor: sees the Audit log and can verify it, but cannot manage users.
      await firstSignIn(page, request, AUDITOR);
      u = COPY[await uiLang(page)];
      await expect(page.getByRole('link', { name: u.navUsers, exact: true })).toHaveCount(0);
      await openNav(page, u.navAudit);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: u.auditHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: u.verifyIntegrity }).click();
      await expect(page.getByText(u.intact)).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: u.addUser })).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.goto('/users');
      await expect(page.getByText(either('noAccess'))).toBeVisible();
    });

    test('new screens have no serious accessibility violations', async ({ page, request }) => {
      await page.goto('/');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.wizardHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await expectAccessible(page, 'setup');

      await runWizard(page, request, lang);
      await expectAccessible(page, 'home');

      await openNav(page, c.navUsers);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.usersHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await expectAccessible(page, 'users');

      await openNav(page, c.navTokens);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.tokensHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await expectAccessible(page, 'tokens');

      await openNav(page, c.navAccount);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.accountHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await expectAccessible(page, 'account');

      await openNav(page, c.navAudit);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: c.auditHeading })).toBeVisible();
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: c.verifyIntegrity }).click();
      await expect(page.getByText(c.intact)).toBeVisible();
      await expectAccessible(page, 'audit');

      await signOut(page);
      await expectAccessible(page, 'login');
    });
  });
}
```

```ts
import { test, expect, type Page } from '@playwright/test';
import { COPY, resetInstall } from './helpers';

function collectProblems(page: Page): string[] {
  const problems: string[] = [];
  page.on('console', (m) => {
    // A signed-out app gets 401 from /api/v1/auth/session; Chromium logs that as a failed resource.
    if (m.type() === 'error' && !m.text().startsWith('Failed to load resource')) problems.push(m.text());
  });
  page.on('pageerror', (e) => problems.push(e.message));
  return problems;
}

test.beforeEach(async ({ request }) => {
  await resetInstall(request);
});

test('a fresh install lands on the setup wizard without CSP or script errors', async ({ page }) => {
  const problems = collectProblems(page);
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/setup$/);
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: COPY.en.wizardHeading })).toBeVisible();
  await expect(page.locator('html')).toHaveAttribute('lang', 'en');
  expect(problems.filter((p) => /Content Security Policy/i.test(p))).toEqual([]);
  expect(problems).toEqual([]);
});

test.describe('Turkish browser', () => {
  test.use({ locale: 'tr-TR' });
  test('first visit is in Turkish', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/');
    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: COPY.tr.wizardHeading })).toBeVisible();
    await expect(page.locator('html')).toHaveAttribute('lang', 'tr');
  });
});

test('deep links (also with a dot) load the app and unknown API paths return JSON errors', async ({ page, request }) => {
  // The server answers with the SPA (200), not a 404, even with a dot in the last segment;
  // /users/a.b is not a client route, so the app itself shows its "Page not found" screen.
  const deep = await page.goto('/users/a.b');
  expect(deep?.status()).toBe(200);
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Page not found' })).toBeVisible();

  const res = await request.get('/api/v1/nope', { headers: { 'Accept-Language': 'tr' } });
  expect(res.status()).toBe(404);
  const body = await res.json();
  expect(body.error.code).toBe('ALC-SYS-0001');
  expect(body.error.message).toContain('bulunamadı');
});

test('security headers are present', async ({ request }) => {
  const res = await request.get('/');
  const h = res.headers();
  expect(h['content-security-policy']).toMatch(/style-src 'self' 'nonce-[A-Za-z0-9+/=]+'/);
  expect(h['strict-transport-security']).toBe('max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains');
  expect(h['x-frame-options']).toBe('DENY');
});

test('the session cookie is never readable by scripts', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: COPY.en.wizardHeading })).toBeVisible();
  expect(await page.evaluate(() => document.cookie)).not.toContain('alice');
});
```

In `docs/superpowers/specs/alice-v1/04-security.md`, replace the whole SEC-035 bullet with:
```markdown
- **SEC-035** The React app never uses `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; ESLint rule enforced; translation strings are plain text with interpolation only. The build emits no inline scripts. Styles come from the bundled CSS file and from Mantine's single runtime `<style>` element, which carries the per-request CSP nonce (SEC-033); component style props are applied by React through the CSSOM, which CSP does not restrict, so no `unsafe-inline` is needed. [T]
```

Replace the whole SEC-041 bullet with:
```markdown
- **SEC-041** AAD binds each ciphertext to its location. Payload layer: `table || '/' || row_id || '/' || column`. DEK-wrap layer: the same string plus `'/' || key_version`. Master-key rotation re-wraps only DEKs (recomputing the DEK-wrap AAD with the new version) and never touches payload ciphertext; moving ciphertext between rows or columns fails to decrypt. [T]
```

Append to `docs/superpowers/specs/alice-v1/00-decisions.md`:
```markdown
- R35 (Phase 1, approved with the Phase 1 plan) Accepted Phase 1 implementation decisions: SQL is hand-written with pgx and parameterized queries in each module, and `api/openapi.yaml` stays hand-maintained with strict JSON decoding (unknown fields rejected, 1 MiB cap) plus route/spec consistency tests, instead of sqlc, oapi-codegen and openapi-typescript (revisit when the schema stabilises); the application connects as `alice_app` (SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on normal tables, SELECT/INSERT only on `audit_log` and `master_key_versions`) while migrations run as the owner role through `ALICE_MIGRATE_DATABASE_URL`; `audit_log.seq` is assigned by the application (max+1 under the audit advisory lock) instead of an identity column; the CSRF token is an HMAC of the session token under the HKDF key `alice/csrf/v1`, so `sessions` has no `csrf_token_hash` column and gains `kind` (`pending`|`full`); login lockout state lives on `users.failed_logins`/`users.locked_until` and the `login_attempts` table is deferred; HKDF uses the Go standard library `crypto/hkdf`; the master key file is created on first start only when `master_key_versions` is empty (install.sh pre-creates it from Phase 8) and Alice refuses to start when key versions exist but the file is missing or different; the setup-code hash is kept in `settings` and created with `alice setup-token --reset` until install.sh exists; the Phase 1 wizard ends after the recovery passphrase and later phases insert their steps before "Finish"; the TOTP enrollment QR code is rendered server-side by `pquerna/otp` (through its `boombuler/barcode` dependency) and shown as a PNG data URI; API tokens carry exactly one scope (`read` or `operate`); user actions use `/users/{id}/actions/<verb>`; the frontend adds React Router 7 (React 18 compatible) and msw 2; a test-only `e2e` build tag adds `/test/clock`, `/test/reset` and `/test/setup-token` and is never shipped.
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the first-run guide**

`docs/first-run.md`:
````markdown
# First Run: the Setup Wizard

This guide walks you through the Alice setup wizard. Allow 10 minutes.

## Before you begin

- A phone with an authenticator app (Microsoft Authenticator, Google Authenticator or any app that supports
  "time-based codes").
- A place to store 10 recovery codes (a password manager or a printed sheet in a safe).
- A recovery passphrase of at least 16 characters, written on paper and stored somewhere safe. It protects your
  backups. If you lose it, nobody (including the vendor) can open your backups.
- The one-time setup code. On a development installation, run `make setup-token`. On a server installation,
  run `sudo docker compose exec alice /usr/local/bin/alice setup-token --reset` in the Alice directory. The code
  looks like `K7QX2M-9TPD4R-WB5H8N-3VZC6A` and stops working as soon as the administrator exists.

## Steps

1. Open `https://<server-address>` in your browser. Accept the temporary certificate warning. Alice shows
   **Set up Alice**.
   Expected: the page asks for the **Setup code**.
2. Type the setup code and choose **Continue**. After five wrong codes from one computer, the wizard waits
   15 minutes before it accepts new attempts.
   Expected: **Choose your language**.
3. Choose English or Türkçe and choose **Continue**. This is the default language for everyone; each person can
   change it later.
   Expected: **Create the administrator account**.
4. Enter a username, your full name, an optional email address and a password of at least 12 characters.
   Alice refuses common passwords from a list of 100,000 leaked passwords and tells you why.
   Expected: **Set up your authenticator app** with a QR code.
5. Scan the QR code with your authenticator app (or type the text code shown below it), then type the
   **6-digit code** from the app and choose **Verify**.
   Expected: **Save your recovery codes**.
6. Save the 10 recovery codes. Each code signs you in once if you lose your phone. Tick **I saved these codes in
   a safe place** and choose **Continue**. You are now signed in as the administrator, and the setup code no
   longer works.
   Expected: **Choose a recovery passphrase**.
7. Type the recovery passphrase twice, tick **I stored the passphrase safely** and choose **Save passphrase**.
   If more than five minutes passed since you verified your code, Alice asks for a new 6-digit code first
   (**Confirm it's you**).
   Expected: **Alice is ready**.
8. Choose **Go to the dashboard**.

If you close the browser after step 6, sign in normally (password, then the code from your app). Alice shows a
red banner **Finish setting up: choose a recovery passphrase.** until you complete step 7.

## If something goes wrong

Every error message shows a code such as `ALC-SET-0001`. Look it up in [troubleshooting.md](troubleshooting.md).
````

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the administrator guide (Phase 1 sections)**

`docs/admin-guide.md`:
````markdown
# Administrator Guide

This guide covers day-to-day administration. Sections for targets, certificates, SSH keys, alerts and SSO are
added in later releases.

## Users and roles

Alice has three roles:

| Role | Can do | Cannot do |
|---|---|---|
| Administrator | Everything: users, settings, audit log, API tokens of other people | — |
| Operator | View inventory; run discovery, deploy certificates and add or remove SSH keys (from later releases) | Manage users, see the audit log, change settings |
| Auditor | View everything read-only, view and export the audit log, verify its integrity | Change anything |

The full list of which role may call which API operation is in [authz-matrix.md](authz-matrix.md).

### Add a person

1. Open **Users** and choose **Add user**.
2. Enter the username, full name, optional email, **Role** and an **Initial password** (at least 12 characters).
3. Choose **Create user**. If you have not confirmed your identity in the last five minutes, Alice asks for a
   6-digit code (**Confirm it's you**).
4. Give the person their username and initial password through a separate channel. At their first sign-in they
   scan a QR code with their authenticator app and save their own recovery codes.

Expected: the person appears in the list; the two-factor column shows "Not set up yet" until their first sign-in.

### Change a role, disable a person, reset their MFA or password

Open **Users**, choose **Manage** on the person's row and choose the action. Changing a role, disabling a person,
resetting their MFA or password, and **Sign out everywhere** end all their current sessions immediately.

Alice never lets you remove the last enabled local administrator (disable or change role). This keeps a
break-glass account available.

## Multi-factor authentication and recovery codes

Every local account must use an authenticator app. Each code works once. If a person loses their phone they sign
in with one of their recovery codes (each works once) and then create new recovery codes on **My account**.
If they have no recovery codes left, an administrator resets their MFA on **Users**; if the only administrator is
locked out, the server operator runs `alice admin reset-mfa <username>` (see
[operator-guide.md](operator-guide.md)).

## Sign-in protection

After 5 wrong attempts (password or code) in a row, Alice makes the account wait a little longer before each new
attempt (1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 seconds). After 10, the account is blocked for 15 minutes. During the wait the
message stays "Incorrect username or password." even for the right password, so an attacker learns nothing.
Every sign-in, failure and block is in the audit log.

## API tokens

**API tokens** lets each person create tokens for scripts. Choose a name, the access (**read** or **read and
operate**; auditors can only create read tokens) and how many days the token is valid (at most 365). The token is
shown once; copy it immediately. Scripts send it as `Authorization: Bearer alice_…`. Tokens cannot manage users,
settings or tokens. Revoking a token stops it at once. Administrators can revoke anyone's token.

## Audit log

**Audit log** (administrators and auditors) lists every sign-in, failure, denied request and change, newest
first. Each entry is linked to the previous one with a SHA-256 hash, so changing or deleting an entry breaks the
chain.

- **Verify integrity** checks the whole chain. Expected: "The audit log is intact." If it reports a problem at an
  entry, keep a copy of the database and contact your security team.
- **Export CSV** / **Export JSON** download the whole log with its verification result. Exports are recorded in
  the audit log.
- Record the head hash shown after verification somewhere outside Alice (for example in a ticket) from time to
  time. Syslog forwarding arrives in a later release.
````

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the operator guide (Phase 1 sections)**

`docs/operator-guide.md`:
````markdown
# Operator Guide

Commands run inside the `alice` container. On a development machine use the `make` targets; on a server use
`sudo docker compose exec alice /usr/local/bin/alice <command>` in the Alice directory.

## Commands

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `alice setup-token --reset` | Prints a new one-time setup code. Works only while no administrator exists. Development: `make setup-token`. |
| `alice audit verify` | Verifies the audit hash chain. Exit code 0 and "intact" when the chain is complete; exit code 1 and the first broken entry otherwise. The check is itself recorded in the audit log. |
| `alice admin reset-mfa <username>` | Removes the person's authenticator and recovery codes and ends their sessions; they enrol again at the next sign-in. Use it when the only administrator lost both phone and recovery codes. Recorded in the audit log as done by `cli`. |
| `alice migrate` | Applies database migrations and exits. |

## Database roles

Alice uses two PostgreSQL roles:

- The **owner** role (`ALICE_MIGRATE_DATABASE_URL`) creates and changes tables at startup.
- The **application** role `alice_app` (`ALICE_DATABASE_URL`) serves requests. It can only add and read audit log
  entries, never change or delete them. A database trigger blocks changes even for the owner.

If `ALICE_MIGRATE_DATABASE_URL` is empty, Alice uses the application URL for both and logs a warning.

## Master key

Alice encrypts secrets (for example authenticator secrets) with a 256-bit master key in `master.key` inside the
keys directory (`/opt/alice/keys`). On the very first start Alice creates it (mode 0400). Afterwards:

- If the file is missing, Alice refuses to start ("the master key file is missing but this database already holds
  encrypted data"). Restore the file from your backup; Alice never creates a new key over existing data.
- If the file is different from the one the database was created with, Alice refuses to start ("the master key
  does not match this database").
- If other users can read the file, Alice refuses to start. Fix it with `chmod 0400 master.key`.

The server disk must use full-disk encryption: anyone who can read both the database and this file can decrypt the
secrets.

## Development stack reset

`make reset` deletes the development database and keys volumes and starts a fresh installation. Use it after
pulling Phase 1 onto a Phase 0 development stack (the new `alice_app` role is created only on a fresh database).
````

- [ ] **Step 6: Add the sign-in symptoms to the troubleshooting guide**

In `docs/troubleshooting.md`, insert directly after the first paragraph (before `## ALC-SYS-0001`):
````markdown
## Cannot sign in

- **"Incorrect username or password." although the password is right.** After 5 wrong attempts Alice makes the
  account wait a few seconds; after 10 it blocks the account for 15 minutes. Wait and try again. An administrator
  can see `auth.lockout` in the audit log.
- **Lost the phone with the authenticator app.** Choose **Use a recovery code** on the sign-in page. Without
  recovery codes, ask an administrator to reset your MFA (**Users**). If you are the only administrator, the
  server operator runs `alice admin reset-mfa <username>` ([operator-guide.md](operator-guide.md)).
- **"Your session ended."** Sessions end after 30 minutes without activity and always after 12 hours. Sign in
  again.
- **The setup wizard says the setup code is wrong or the session expired.** Create a new code with
  `alice setup-token --reset` (development: `make setup-token`). After five wrong codes the wizard waits
  15 minutes (`ALC-SET-0002`).
- **Alice does not start: "the master key file is missing" / "does not match this database".** See
  [operator-guide.md](operator-guide.md#master-key).
- **Alice does not start: "the database server … rejected the user name or password".** The database password in
  the configuration is wrong, or (development) a Phase 0 database volume lacks the new `alice_app` role: run
  `make reset`.

````

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the changelog**

`CHANGELOG.md`:
````markdown
# Changelog

## Unreleased — Phase 1: Core

### Added
- Setup wizard: one-time setup code, language, administrator account with mandatory authenticator app and
  10 recovery codes, recovery passphrase (generates the backup key pair).
- Local sign-in with Argon2id passwords (12–128 characters, 100,000-entry leaked-password list), mandatory TOTP,
  recovery codes, progressive delay and 15-minute lockout, step-up confirmation for sensitive changes.
- Sessions (`__Host-alice_session`, 30 minutes idle, 12 hours absolute) with CSRF protection and rate limits.
- Roles Administrator, Operator and Auditor enforced on every API route from one permission table
  (`docs/authz-matrix.md`).
- User management, personal API tokens (read or operate scope, at most 365 days).
- Tamper-evident audit log with viewer, CSV/JSON export and integrity verification (`alice audit verify`).
- Envelope encryption (AES-256-GCM, per-secret key) with a master key file; the application database role cannot
  change or delete audit entries.
- CLI: `alice setup-token --reset`, `alice audit verify`, `alice admin reset-mfa <username>`.

### Changed
- `/api/v1/version` now requires sign-in.
- Database errors for a wrong password or a missing database stop startup immediately with a clear message.
- Development stack: separate `alice_app` database role; run `make reset` once after upgrading from Phase 0.

### Known issues
- See "Known Limitations" in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-30-phase-1-core.md`.
````

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the security review note**

`docs/security/reviews/phase-1.md`:
````markdown
# Security Review — Phase 1 (Core: local auth + TOTP, RBAC, audit, secret store)

Scope (spec §17.2, Phase 1): crypto design, sessions and CSRF, MFA, envelope encryption, audit chain, redaction.
Exit criteria: authz matrix test passes; leak test passes; audit chain verifies; peer crypto review sign-off; no
High finding open.

## Evidence

| # | Check | Command | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Authorization matrix over every route and identity (§25.1) | `go test ./internal/api -run TestAuthzMatrix -count=1` and `-run TestRoutePermissionsMatchSpec` and `-run TestNoRouteBypassesTable` | ok |
| 2 | `docs/authz-matrix.md` is current | `go test ./internal/api -run TestAuthzMatrixDocIsCurrent -count=1` | ok |
| 3 | Secret-leak suite S-01..S-05, S-08, S-10 | `go test -tags integration ./internal/api -run TestSecretsNeverLeak -count=1` and `go test ./internal/api -tags integration -run TestOpenAPIResponsesExposeNoSecretFields -count=1` | ok |
| 4 | Session, CSRF, rate limit, lockout (A-01..A-09, R-01..R-09) | `go test -tags integration ./internal/api -run 'Test[AR]0' -count=1` | ok |
| 5 | Audit chain F-90..F-95, F-97 and tail truncation | `go test -tags integration ./internal/audit -count=1` | ok |

## Crypto design review checklist

- [ ] AES-256-GCM with 96-bit random nonces for payload and DEK wrap; fresh DEK per secret (SEC-040).
- [ ] AAD binds table, row, column and (wrap layer) key version (SEC-041 as amended).
- [ ] Master key 256-bit, file 0400, refuse on wider permissions, never regenerated over existing data (SEC-042).
- [ ] Sub-keys only via HKDF-SHA256 with the four fixed labels; master key never used directly as a MAC key (SEC-043).
- [ ] Argon2id m=64 MiB, t=3, p=2 for passwords; m=64 MiB, t=3, p=4 for the backup key; concurrency bounded.
- [ ] TOTP SHA-1/6 digits/30 s ±1 step, last step stored, replay rejected (SEC-006).
- [ ] Session tokens 256-bit, only SHA-256 stored, `__Host-` cookie, rotation on login, MFA, step-up and password change (SEC-018, SEC-019).
- [ ] CSRF token = HMAC-SHA256(K_csrf, session token), constant-time compare, Origin/Sec-Fetch-Site check (SEC-024).
- [ ] Constant-time comparison for tokens, codes and hashes (SEC-049).
````

## Phase 1 Done When

- `make ci` passes locally (lint, unit, integration, build, i18n-check, web lint and tests, vulnerability and secret scans, e2e stack, hardening check, Playwright EN/TR with axe, image scan).
- The authz matrix, secret-leak, session/CSRF/rate-limit and audit-chain suites pass, and `docs/authz-matrix.md` is current.
- On a fresh release-mode stack (`make reset`, `make setup-token`) the owner verification of spec §26.3 succeeds: admin created via the wizard, TOTP sign-in, the three roles behave differently, the audit chain verifies (UI and `alice audit verify`), and repeated wrong passwords slow down and then block the account.
- `docs/security/reviews/phase-1.md` lists no open High finding.
- The known limitations below are recorded for the owner report.

## Known Limitations (carried to later phases)

- The setup code is created with `alice setup-token --reset` (`make setup-token`); `install.sh` printing it at install time arrives in Phase 8.
- The wizard ends after the recovery passphrase; HTTPS certificate upload, SMTP, license and first target are added by Phases 8, 7, 2 and 3. The temporary certificate is still renewed only at container start.
- Lockout and audit-integrity problems are recorded in the audit log but not emailed or forwarded to syslog (Phase 7), and there is no scheduled daily verification yet (Phase 3 jobs).
- A person created by an administrator is not forced to change the initial password at first sign-in (not required by the spec); the admin guide asks administrators to hand passwords over through a separate channel.
- API tokens have one scope (`read` or `operate`); in Phase 1 no route accepts `operate` beyond what `read` allows, because operate actions arrive with targets in Phase 3.
- In-memory IP and API rate limits reset on restart (R20); the per-account lockout survives restarts.
- PostgreSQL still runs with a writable root filesystem and default capabilities, and database passwords live in environment variables; container hardening of PostgreSQL and secret files arrive with packaging in Phase 8.
- Development stacks created in Phase 0 must be recreated once with `make reset` (the `alice_app` role is created only on a fresh database volume).
